- [x] ~~**`--test-models` was recommending the wrong model, in its own output, on the day the rule changed.**~~ **FIXED August 17, 2026, in the same session it was seen.** Its closing line read `Suggested: echo 'WRITER_MODEL=gemini-3.1-pro-preview'` while listing `gemini-3.7-flash` below it as merely "worth comparing against" — because the helper hardcoded *prefer a pro tier over a flash tier* and then picked the newest **within** that bucket. **On this key that means it recommended the older model with more confidence than the newer one**, and named as "suggested" the exact model Google's migration guide says to move off.
  *The fix is not a better ranking — it is refusing to rank.* It now prints both tiers in version order, names the highest version number usable **across** both, and says in the output that this is a version number and not a recommendation, with the two `--dry-run` commands to settle it by reading. **A helper that cannot know which model writes better Spanish should not be the thing that decides**, and the previous wording was confident enough that a future session would have taken it as an answer.
  *Second-order: it also now prints the `grep` for `WRITER_MODEL`/`CONTENT_MODEL` in both `.env` files, because its `echo ... >>` suggestion is precisely what created the duplicate line closed above.*

## Closed — the August 14, 2026 status-claim audit section, moved here September 2, 2026

***A section of `open-loops.md` that had no open items left in it once its two duplicates were removed — and whose own opening paragraph said so:*** **"nine questions put to Iván the same day and all nine answered and closed — nothing from this pass is left open."** *It stayed in the live list for nineteen days on the strength of two items that each described themselves as duplicates of an item forty lines above.* **A section that finishes its job gets retired, not maintained** *— the repo's standing rule, applied for the first time to a section rather than to a whole document.* *Its content is worth keeping and is reproduced verbatim: the audit's summary, the correction to its own loudest finding (the false-alarm cache), and the two structural proposals Iván approved and applied — deleting `infrastructure.html`'s competing open-items list, and stamping every `Status:` line with a `verified:` date.*

### Opened by the status-claim audit (August 14, 2026) — all are *verification* items, not build items

*First real run of the one-off audit in `kb-hygiene-prompt.md`. Twenty-one stale state claims corrected across seventeen files; nine questions put to Iván the same day and **all nine answered and closed — nothing from this pass is left open**. Both structural proposals were approved and applied. Ten new failure modes written into `kb-hygiene-prompt.md` so the weekly pass inherits them.*

> ***One correction to the audit itself, resolved August 17, 2026 — its loudest finding was wrong.*** *The 🚨 item below was filed on Aug 14 as a cache fault in Iván's stack and turned out, three days later, to be a cache in **Claude's own fetch tool**. It is left in full rather than deleted, because a pass whose entire premise is "verify against a different source" producing a false alarm by trusting its own instrument is the most useful thing this run generated. **Rule added: when a live check disagrees with the repo, suspect the check before filing the finding.*** *Note also that the finding sat open for three days purely because the answer needed one `curl` from Iván —* **a 🚨 whose resolution costs thirty seconds should not wait on a session boundary.**


> ***Both structural proposals approved by Iván and APPLIED, August 14, 2026.***
>
> **(a) `infrastructure.html`'s "Open items / not yet done" list is deleted.** *It was a second copy of this file, and **four of its ten entries were provably wrong**: a storefront crawl recorded as paused mid-flight while its output file sat in `data/`; `plans_raw`'s "4 duplicate plan_ids" deduped July 29; HubSpot listed as both a planned MCP integration and a system "running side-by-side" three weeks after decommission; and the **"6 plans marked published 404" ghost** the project instructions record as having been retired **twice** — it kept coming back because that line was never struck. Two more entries were closed long ago and kept regenerating questions for Iván.* **Deleted rather than struck**, per this repo's own rule that a *retracted* claim gets removed while a *superseded* one gets struck — struck-through text still reads as text. The two genuinely-open items were promoted here first.
>
> **(b) Every `Status:` line on a runbook or brief now carries a `verified: <date>` stamp.** *None of the ten carried one before, so a reader could not tell a line written this morning from one written three weeks ago — which is exactly how a "NOT LIVE" survives eight days past the deploy. **The date is the cheap part; what it buys is the reader's ability to distrust the line without having to re-verify it.***

## Closed — four duplicate items removed from `open-loops.md`, September 2, 2026

***Four open items that were each a second copy of another item in the same file.*** *All four were labelled as duplicates* ***in their own text***, *by the sessions that created them — and all four were then carried as live work anyway.*

**Two said so outright:** `plan-tracker-bigquery` *and* `MCP tool integrations` *each ended with* **"(Duplicate of the entry in the promoted-from-infra list above — that one is the owner.)"** *and had been carried as four items since August 14, 2026 — nineteen days, in a file with a stated WIP limit of one.* *Removing them empties the "Opened by the status-claim audit (August 14, 2026)" section, which is the correct outcome: that pass's own summary says* **"all nine answered and closed — nothing from this pass is left open."** *It was true, and the section stayed populated by the two duplicates alone.*

**The other two are a different and more interesting failure:** *the members-auth compose build context and the `v` cache-bust filter. Each had its title struck through and marked* **"(original entry, superseded above)"** *— but the box was left unchecked and the full body left in place, so each still rendered as an open item with a paragraph of live-sounding detail.* ***The superseding entries were both already ticked closed, and both were swept into this archive in the same pass — which is what exposed these: an "original entry, superseded above" that no longer has anything above it.*** **Striking a title is not closing an item.** *Three signals said done — strikethrough, the word superseded, a closed owner — and the one signal that any tooling or any reader actually counts, the checkbox, said open.*

*Reproduced verbatim:*

- [ ] ~~**Fix the members-auth compose build context**~~ *(original entry, superseded above)* *(Found Aug 12, 2026 by the first run of `automation/audit-runtime-paths.sh`.)* The live file still reads `build: ./auth_service`, so the auth service is **still** built from `/root/.members-auth/`, outside the clone, and `automation/members-area/auth_service/app.py` remains a reference copy that changes nothing when edited. `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §13 recorded this as fixed on Aug 10 and has been corrected in place. One line to change, then `docker compose up -d --build`, **then re-run the grep and paste the output into §13** — the whole point of the correction is that a runtime-path fix isn't done until the runtime has been re-inspected.

- [ ] ~~**Decide whether the `v` cache-bust filter should fail loud**~~ *(original entry, superseded above)* *(Opened Aug 12, 2026, created by the Caddyfile change in the same session — logged rather than decided unilaterally, because it can break a deploy.)* `.eleventy.js`'s `v` filter currently catches a missing file, logs `[cache-bust] could not hash … — serving unversioned`, and emits the bare URL. That was the right trade when an unversioned URL merely risked staleness. **`Cache-Control: immutable` changed the consequence:** an asset that slips through unversioned is now pinned in browsers for a year with no bust mechanism, and the only warning is a line in build output nobody reads. The fix is one `throw` — a broken build is loud, recoverable in seconds, and strictly better than a year-long cache poisoning. The argument against is that a deploy fails on a missing asset that might be cosmetic. Iván's call. Current state is safe: a clean build has **0** unversioned css/js refs.

- [ ] **`plan-tracker-bigquery` still on the decommissioned Python 3.9 runtime.** Confirmed still open by Iván, Aug 14, 2026. Cloud Function, 2nd gen; source archived at `automation/pixel-tracker/main.py`. Migrate to 3.12. *(Duplicate of the entry in the promoted-from-infra list above — that one is the owner.)*

- [ ] **MCP tool integrations — Google Workspace, and Twenty via the community MCP server.** Confirmed still not started by Iván, Aug 14, 2026. *(Duplicate of the entry in the promoted-from-infra list above — that one is the owner.)*

Two comeback weeks", numbered for a list that did not come with it; the numeral was stripped rather than left to render as an orphan.* ✅ ***And the cause is fixed, not just the output — confirmed September 3, 2026 by reading `research_agent.py` in the run path.*** *`screen_ideas()` scores every proposed title against the published corpus with IDF-weighted term overlap, **drops** clones at ≥0.45 before Iván sees them, and **annotates** anything ≥0.28 with its two nearest published articles in the `rationale` field the review page already renders. It also compares ideas against each other within a single batch — which is what would have caught the Sep 2 pair written on the same day.* ***The header comment gets the reasoning exactly right and independently reaches the same finding this session did from the corpus: bodies sit below 0.27 Jaccard, so duplication is only visible in titles and slugs.*** **So these merges will not be undone by the next batch.**

  **Final state: 102 articles → 91. Zero duplicate `planCard` ids, 20 redirect lines, no transKey group left half-retired.**

**Settled: the English athlete.** *Nadine has her English token and the members area. Here there was no owning doc to grep: the send happened in WhatsApp.* 🔑 **A repo cannot be checked against work that never touches it. That is an argument for telling it, not for checking harder.**

   **Legacy-rate athletes get the same $50 and should be asked first** *(decided Aug 12, 2026)* — the reward is priced against the incoming athlete at $149, not against what the referrer pays, and the sub-$99 rates went to the earliest signups, so **today's legacy athletes are today's longest-tenured ones** (2024-H2 held 73% to twelve months vs 17–30% since). **The ask must not mention their rate**, though: it turns a gift into a comparison and makes a $75 athlete calculate a number that pricing rule 5 depends on nobody calculating. Reasoning and the reframe that avoids it: `sales-playbook.md` B9.

   **What makes this a build rather than a message.** Four pieces, and the third is the one that will be underestimated:
   1. **Attribution.** `leadSource: REFERRAL` already exists on Twenty's Person; **who referred whom does not.** Needs one field (`referredBy`), or the payout depends on Iván remembering.
   2. **The due-for-an-ask list.** The GBP review ask has the same shape and is **already manual** (`social-proof-and-reviews.md`: ~22 active athletes never asked, ~14 due next cycle). **Build one list, not two** — an athlete due a review ask and an athlete due a referral ask are nearly the same query, and two lists will disagree within a month.
   3. **Detecting the referred athlete's second payment.** The lifecycle workflow is IMAP-triggered on TrainingPeaks emails, and it is built around *first* subscription and cancellation. **Whether a recurring-payment email arrives at all is unverified** — check before designing the payout trigger around it. If it doesn't, the fallback is a date calculation off `coachingStartDate`, which is fine but is a different build.
   4. **Payout + ledger.** Manual gift-card purchase; needs a record of owed/paid, or it gets forgotten and the program dies quietly on its first payout.

### 🚨 The ninth item: closed in the world, never ticked, and sitting in NOW

**The activation-engine item above was `- [ ]` while its own first line read "FIXED September 4, 2026" and its `[x]` child read "CHECKED September 5 — CLEAN."** *Both halves were done. Nothing was open. It sat in the NOW section that every session reads first, for four days.*

***This is the exact inverse of the September 5 finding, and the pair of them describe one defect from both ends:*** *that pass found a **parent left open while its children were ticked** (`subscription-lifecycle-automation.json`, moved above) and wrote the rule "a parent item is not closed by its children being ticked." **Here the children were ticked, the parent's own text announced the fix, and still nothing closed it.*** 🔑 **So the rule generalises past parents and children: nothing in this file closes itself, and a completion written into an item's prose is not a state change.** *The only thing that closes an item is a person moving it here.*

## Closed — September 2, 2026 (weekly hygiene pass)

- [x] ~~**🚨 Unmap `id` from the notifier's Postgres insert.**~~ **FIXED AND VERIFIED September 3, 2026.** *`id` mapped to a literal `0` on an INSERT into a `bigserial PRIMARY KEY`: the first row took `id = 0` and every later error would have died on a duplicate key —* ***silently***, *because the Telegram node runs in parallel and keeps working.* **Verified the only way that proves anything here: two consecutively forced errors, producing ids 1 and 2 rather than one row and a swallowed failure.** *Repo copy mirrored.* ⚠️ ***Found by reading the export, not by anything failing*** *— the smoke test had already passed, because one insert is exactly the number that works.* **That is the shape of four separate defects this session: `gbp_keywords`' destructive write, the GA4 rule that never matched, the unreachable collector, and this. Each worked once or looked green, and each failed in the half nobody watches.**

- [x] ~~**Export `Error notifier (global)` from n8n into `automation/`.**~~ **DONE September 3, 2026** — `automation/n8n-error-notifier-workflow.json`. *The workflow every other workflow names in its Error Workflow field finally has a reference copy; its absence is what let a session read the orphaned collector as the error mechanism and write the wiring instruction naming it.* **`automation/n8n-error-collector-workflow.json` retired to `_to_delete/` in the same pass** *— the live workflow is archived and the notifier now owns the insert, so keeping the file would leave two copies of one mechanism.* ⚠️ ***Reading the export immediately produced a defect the smoke test could not have caught*** *— see the `id`-mapping item in the Backlog. A workflow that passes one test is not a workflow that works.*

- [x] ~~**The 08:00 digest has never delivered an email.**~~ **CONFIRMED WORKING September 3, 2026** — *Iván forced a run and received the email with one error in it.* *That is the last link in the chain verified end to end: failure → Error Trigger → notifier → `n8n_errors` row → digest → inbox.* **Both channels now do different jobs on purpose: Telegram interrupts, the digest batches, and neither is a copy of the other.**

- [x] ~~**n8n failed executions have no queryable store / the error email has never delivered.**~~ **FIXED September 3, 2026, and verified by a deliberate failure.** *Root cause: two workflows existed for one slot. Every workflow's Error Workflow field named `Error notifier (global)` (Telegram, many executions); `Error Collector (global)` (the Postgres insert) was named nowhere, so it had* **zero executions ever** *— it was unreachable rather than broken.* **`n8n_errors` therefore stayed empty, and the 08:00 digest ran green every morning with nothing to send** — *which is exactly the trade-off the Aug 8 build log wrote down and accepted: "a silent digest is indistinguishable from a broken one." It took a month to notice.* **Fix: the `Store Error in Postgres` node moved into the notifier, parallel to the Telegram so the alert never waits on Postgres.** *Smoke-tested with a temporary active webhook workflow that throws — row written, Telegram delivered, both channels confirmed in one run.* ⚠️ ***The test had to be a production execution: the Error Trigger does not fire for manual runs.*** *Close #1's "unmeasured" row for failed executions becomes a real monthly count from the October close — **and zero only means something now that something has proven it can be non-zero**.*

- [x] ~~**Upgrade n8n 2.33.4 → 2.35.4.**~~ **ALREADY DONE — and done 13 days before anyone noticed.** *`docker ps -a` shows `n8n` running `n8nio/n8n:2.35.4`, up 13 days, and `n8n-old` exited 13 days ago on `2.33.4` — exactly the rename-don't-remove rollback the item prescribed.* ⚠️ ***The item carried a full plan, a triage of all nine advisories and a verification order, and stayed open through two hygiene passes after the work was finished.*** *Nothing re-read it on the day it was done.* *`n8n-old` can now be removed: 13 days is far past any plausible rollback window, it holds a stale copy of the SQLite state, and it is one of the `CHECK` rows the runtime-path audit will keep reporting.*

- [x] ~~**No bulk audit of leads affected by the July 31 email-nurture and multi-item execution bugs.**~~ **CLOSED September 2, 2026, Iván: "very very small, likely zero."** *Corrections were made ad hoc at the time; the residue is not worth a query.*

- [x] ~~**Why does `/pt/planos/ciclismo/` have zero impressions when its two sibling hubs have 98 and 30?**~~ **ANSWERED September 2, 2026, and neither of the two hypotheses was right.** *Not technical — built, in the sitemap, internally linked from 96 pages, identical to the working hubs.* ***And not vocabulary either: my `planilha` hypothesis is disproven.*** *The marathon hub matches `plano de treino maratona`, `plano de treinos maratona` and `plano treino maratona` — Google is matching the site's own wording exactly, so the hubs are not written in the wrong Portuguese.* **The actual answer is that there is no Portuguese cycling demand in the data at all: of the 16 Brazilian queries the entire site has ever appeared for, not one is about cycling.** *The page has never been judged relevant to anything because nothing relevant has been searched — with 9 PT cycling plans against a market full of large sites, that is unremarkable rather than broken.* ⚠️ **Recorded as a lesson about diagnosis: a page with zero impressions is invisible to every check that reads impressions. The question was only answerable by looking at what the *neighbours* rank for.**

- [x] ~~**GSC average position must come off the scoreboard.**~~ **DONE September 2, 2026.** *`monthly-close-runbook.md`'s metric inventory now reads "impressions, clicks, near-miss query count (positions 5–20), coverage errors" — the near-miss band is already the view `gsc_near_miss`, so nothing had to be built. The reasoning is recorded in the runbook next to the row, because the trap generalises: an impression-weighted average moves against you exactly when you start ranking for more queries.*

- [x] ~~**Formalise a monthly close**~~ **CLOSED September 2, 2026, Iván.** *Close #1 shipped September 1 — this was its own trigger item, still sitting open in the section named after it. Iván: "crazy this is open."* **An item that produces a deliverable does not close itself when the deliverable ships.**

  > Original item:

  **Formalise a monthly close: the list of paying athletes at every month end.** *(Iván, Aug 12, 2026 — his proposal. **Trigger: formalise by end of August 2026**, i.e. the August close is the first one.)* Came out of the reconciliation gap between `tenure-analysis.md` and `triaperformance-business-overview.md`, whose real cause is that **one is a June snapshot and the other is a history — they were never going to agree except in the month the snapshot was taken.** A dated monthly file makes retention and revenue reproducible month over month and gives every doc a figure to *point at* instead of a copy to maintain, which is this repo's recurring failure mode.
    **Design, so the first one isn't reworked:** one row per paying athlete per month close — `athlete_id` (same `sha256(normalised name)[:8]` derivation as `data/athlete_tenure.csv`, so it joins for free), `source`, `monthly_rate`, `channel`. **The rate column is the point**, not a nice-to-have: `tenure-analysis.md` §3's revenue view is currently modelled from a price ladder, and this is what turns it into a measurement. **No names in the repo** — same standing rule as every other export.
    *Small and self-closing — it becomes a five-minute monthly habit, and after two closes it also gives net revenue retention, which nothing currently measures.*
  
    ***Update, August 12, 2026 — scoped up, and it now has a home doc: `monthly-close-runbook.md`.*** *Iván's ask was broader than the athlete roster — athletes/acquisition/churn, costs, GA4, GBP, Instagram, Ahrefs/GSC warnings, leads and sales conversion, **and a P&L**. The runbook owns the design; this item stays open only until the first close is run.*
    **What the runbook adds beyond the roster design above:** a **P&L structure** (costs have never been totalled in any form — that inventory is the largest piece of Month 0 work); **metric tiering** (monthly / quarterly / annual, because at 2–3 signups a month a monthly conversion *rate* is noise and reporting noise trains you to ignore the report); and the standing rule that **after close #2 no document states a current business figure — it cites the dated close file.**
    **Second argument for it, found while writing the runbook and worth stating on its own:** `triaperformance-business-overview.md` carries passive plans at **$847/mo**, which is June 2026's earnings to the cent ($846.81, the best month in `data/plan_sales.csv`). **July was $301.63 on 9 units against June's 23.** Nobody typed a wrong number — a snapshot was taken on a peak and quoted as a rate for two months. *The trailing-3-month column in the close exists specifically to make that mistake impossible, and it is not optional.*
    **Month 0 blockers, in order — two of them cannot be reconstructed later and should not wait:** (1) the three-month cost inventory from Mercury/Stripe/cards; (2) **`agreementDate` and `churnReason` fields in Twenty** — `signup_date` is the start date the athlete *chose*, so time-to-close is not computable from it, and "completed their goal" vs "left dissatisfied" is invisible in the current data; (3) a canonical name spelling, since `athlete_id` is name-derived and a second spelling silently splits an athlete in two. Detail: `monthly-close-runbook.md` §6.
  
    ***Update, September 1, 2026 — the roster half of close #1 is BUILT and clean. Four of the five Month 0 pieces are closed; only the cost inventory remains, and it is now the single thing gating the P&L.***
    ✅ **`data/monthly_close/2026-08-roster.csv` exists** — 47 rows, `twenty_id` + `tpId`, **no names or emails**. 42 charged, 5 August churns. **August: gross MRR $3,962.19, net of platform fees $3,222.88** (CoachMatch 33 / Private 3 / All-Access 1), **5 new, 5 churned**, and 3 more already dated to churn in September.
    ✅ **Blocker 2 dissolved rather than built.** *`agreementDate` was never needed:* **Twenty's `signUpDate` is written from the hire/payment email and already IS the day the athlete said yes.** *The trap §4 warned about belongs to a different field with a near-identical name — `signup_date` in `athlete_tenure.csv`, which came from TrainingPeaks and* ***is*** *athlete-chosen. Two systems, two fields, opposite meanings.* **`churnReason` + `churnReasonDetails` built and backfilled** across every churn.
    ✅ **Blocker 5 dissolved too, and its premise was wrong.** *A canonical name spelling does not fix a name-derived key, because* **the athlete controls the name.** `tpId` replaces the hash. *Proof arrived in the data itself: one athlete is `Paula` in Twenty and `Padua` in TrainingPeaks.*
    ✅ **Blocker 3 (the 40-vs-30/5 reconciliation) answered by the export rather than argued.** *39 TP export rows − Iván − the All-Access subscriber − one churned-but-still-attached Private athlete = **37**, matching `athlete_tenure.csv`.* ⚠️ ***And the export was then retired as a source:*** *TrainingPeaks detaches a churned* **CoachMatch** *athlete but leaves a* **Private** *one attached until deleted, so presence proves nothing and absence proves it only on one channel.* **Twenty's `churnDate` is the authority.**
    ⏳ **Only blocker 1 is open: the three-month cost inventory.** *It is now the sole thing standing between this and a real P&L — Iván's call was a full P&L in close #1.*
    **Ten fields were added to Twenty to make this work** (`tpId`, `planCustom`, `listPrice`, `monthlyRate`, `billingChannel`, `commissionPercentage`, `tpPremiumPaid`, `startupFee`, `churnReason`, `churnReasonDetails`, plus `sex`, `dateOfBirth`, `subStartDate`, `revenueStatus`), **`planPurchased` was deleted**, and **15 integrity rules now pass with zero failures** against the live export. Design decisions: `monthly-close-runbook.md` §2.1; schema and traps: `contact-form-pipeline-runbook.md`.
    🚨 **Two findings worth carrying, neither of them about athletes.** *(1)* **The first roster query I wrote filtered on `billingChannel is set`, which silently dropped every comp, barter and internal row** — four unpaid athletes and Iván — *and returned a clean-looking 37.* **Filter on `revenueStatus`.** *(2)* **A field renamed in Twenty leaves the old one queryable and empty**: `subscriptionStartDate` survived the move to `subStartDate` and returned null for everyone, and the export looked perfect. ***Same shape as a stale view — present, queryable, silently wrong. Delete the old field, do not just stop using it.***
    ***The number this surfaced that nothing had ever stated: $1,431.80 of discount given in one month, against $5,394 of list value — the book is being delivered at 26.5% off list, and the rate mix is 26 legacy athletes against 10 at full price.***
  
    ***Update, August 17, 2026 — the GBP half is unblocked. Google approved the Business Profile API application*** (support ticket `8-0766000040742`; approval email to `coach@triaperformance.com` the same day). *`monthly-close-runbook.md` §Automation item 7 read "Needs API setup" and its Tier-M row carried GBP as a manual screenshot capture. The approval clears the long-lead half of that — the application itself, which `content-engine-brief.md` warned would take "weeks, not days."*
    **What it unblocks, precisely.** The **Performance API** pull — views, searches, actions, new reviews — which is the metric this item needs. **It is a copy of something already running, not a new pattern:** `automation/analytics/sync_pixel_data.py` (05:00 cron) already authenticates a Google service account, pulls a Google API and writes Postgres; this is the same shape with a different endpoint, exactly as described for the GSC/GA4 instrumentation in LATER. **It does *not* unblock post publishing** — the local-post endpoint is a different surface of the same API family and its availability to this project is unverified. Parked as its own item in NOW.
    **Iván's part, and it is the same two steps as the GSC/GA4 item, so do them once for all three:** enable the API in the `training-plan-analytics` Cloud project, and grant the service account (preferably the existing `pixel-sync-vps`, keeping it to one credential) access to the Business Profile. *Worth doing before the August close, so close #1 carries a pulled GBP row rather than a screenshot — a screenshot cannot be re-derived next month.*

  > ***The other lesson is about where an item is filed, not whether it is filed.*** *This defect was written down the whole time, as* **"`/members/fuerza/` needs EN + PT"** *in the members-i18n backlog — an accurate sentence that describes a translation chore.* **The same fact, written as "we are selling a tool that does not exist in two languages", would not have survived a week in that list.** *Cyclist Core got the second phrasing on Aug 24 and was resolved; this one got the first and sat there. Filing is not neutral: the words chosen decide which queue an item joins.*

Original item, kept intact:*

  > 🚨 **Paste `triaperformance-project-instructions.md` into the project's Custom instructions field. Two minutes, and it is the highest-leverage open item in this file.** *(Found September 2, 2026, weekly hygiene pass, by comparing the instructions this session actually received against the repo mirror.)*
  > ***The repo mirror is 47 index entries. The copy that loads into every session is 38.*** *Fourteen documents exist in the repo and are invisible to the map every session is told to navigate by:* `tenure-analysis.md`, `monthly-close-runbook.md`, `referral-program-brief.md`, `sales-playbook.md`, `security-posture.md`, `zone-magnet-runbook.md`, `lead-magnet-sesiones-por-zona.md`, `activation-matrix.md`, `achilles-brief.md`, `knee-strength-brief.md`, `strength-guide-brief.md`, `cyclist-core-brief.md`, `site/_data/library.json`, `automation/layout-check.js`.
  > ***Why this is worse than an ordinary stale copy, and why it belongs in NOW rather than the small slot:*** *the August 12 decision deliberately emptied the project knowledge and made the index* ***the*** *map — "the index below is the map, the repo is the territory, grep it."* **A session cannot grep for a document it has not been told exists.** *The mirror has been edited as recently as Aug 30 and the field has not been repasted, so the repo did exactly what the rule asks and the last manual step was skipped.*
  > ⚠️ ***Three of the fourteen are load-bearing for questions being asked right now.*** `monthly-close-runbook.md` *and* `tenure-analysis.md` *are the two docs that govern which figures the close owns — the question Iván opened this session with — and* `cyclist-core-brief.md` *is the home doc whose supposed absence was half of the Aug 24 🚨 finding closed today.* **That finding would have been shorter, or not raised at all, if the session that wrote it had been able to see the file.**
  > **Standing fix, not just this paste:** *the index rule already says "if a doc is added, renamed or retired, update this list in the same session." It does not say* ***and remind Iván to paste it***. *`kb-hygiene-prompt.md`'s END OF SESSION step does say that; it fires only on weeks when a doc changed, and the drift accumulated across sessions where none did. **Make the paste reminder unconditional at the end of every hygiene pass**, and diff the received instructions against the mirror as Step 1 — this pass found it in one comparison.*

## Closed — August 31, 2026

- [x] ~~**`gbp_keywords` writes with a bare `DELETE`, and an empty fetch destroys the month.**~~ *(Found August 31, 2026, while checking why one of eight sources returned zero rows on an otherwise clean run. **The zero itself is almost certainly real** — not a defect, and worth saying first.)*
  **Why the zero is not the finding:** `gbp_search_keywords` holds 13 months back to 2025-03 with **0–7 keywords a month**, and `sum(value)` is NULL for six of them — every row thresholded, i.e. Google returning «fewer than N» rather than a count. *2025-12, 2026-02, 2026-03 and 2026-06 are absent entirely.* **On a profile doing 60–96 views a month, a month with no keywords is ordinary.** *Do not re-raise the zero as a bug.*
  🚨 **The finding is the write path.** *`sync_gbp_data.py` runs `DELETE FROM gbp_search_keywords WHERE location_id=%s AND month=%s` unconditionally, then `INSERT`s only `if krows`.* **So a month that returns empty is deleted and replaced with nothing — an empty API response silently destroys stored data.** *This is the exact inverse of the bug fixed the day before on the GA4 side («an upsert cannot REMOVE a row that should no longer exist», resolved by DELETE-window-then-INSERT). **The same session that made writes destructive on purpose in one script left them destructive by accident in another.***
  **The second-order half, and it is the one that is probably already costing data.** *The daily window is a rolling ~7 days, so the loop can only ever reach the current month. Once the window clears the last day of a month — around the 8th — **that month is frozen at whatever incomplete state it was in**, and Google publishes monthly keyword data* ***after*** *the month closes.* **The rolling-window pattern is correct for GSC, which revises recent days, and wrong here, where the source publishes late rather than revising.** *A monthly source needs a monthly re-fetch of the closed month, not a daily re-fetch of the open one.*
  **Suggested fix, one line each:** guard the `DELETE` on `if krows`, and widen the keyword loop to re-fetch the previous month as well as the current one. *Small; the reason to log it rather than do it in passing is that it changes what a green `ok` row means, and this table's whole purpose is that a zero can be trusted.*

  ***Update, August 31, 2026 — BOTH HALVES FIXED IN THE REPO. Not closed: it closes when Iván runs it on the box and reports what the table holds.*** *Done ahead of close #1 deliberately — a close is the first consumer that quotes these numbers outside this repo, and a wrong number that has been published is a different problem from one that has not.*
  **It came out larger than "one line each", and the extra was worth it:**
  - **The empty fetch never deletes**, and the two empty cases are now separated. *Empty with no prior rows is unremarkable and is what Aug 31 returned; empty **with** prior rows keeps the stored rows and **names the month** in `analytics_sync_log.detail`.*
  - **`status` gains a third value, `partial`.** *A run that kept rows because it had nothing better is neither a success nor a failure, and* ***collapsing it into either one is how this bug stayed invisible for as long as it did*** *— it recorded `ok` every night while deleting data.*
  - **The window fix is the half that was already costing data**, and it reframes the defect: *`start` is `latest − 7 days`, correct for daily metrics and wrong for keywords, because* **GSC and the daily metrics *revise* recent days while GBP keywords are *published late*.** *A rolling 7-day window stops reaching a month around the 8th, so each month froze at whatever partial state it held when the window left it.* ***That is the likeliest explanation for the four months missing entirely (2025-12, 2026-02, 2026-03, 2026-06) — not that Google had nothing, but that nothing went back to ask after it did.*** The loop now reaches the **first of the previous month**.
  ***The two fixes turned out to be one fix, and the ordering is the finding.*** *Re-reading a closed month would have been actively dangerous under the old write path — a late re-fetch returning empty would have erased a month that was previously correct.* **The guard is what makes the wider window safe; the wider window is what makes the guard worth having.**
  **New: `tests/test_gbp_keywords_guard.py`** — 16 checks, no VPS, no Postgres, no network, no third-party packages. ***It carries the pre-fix code as a fixture and asserts that the old version fails***, *because a test that passes against both versions is not testing anything.* `monthly-close-runbook.md` §7's rule, implemented rather than quoted.
  ~~**What is left is Iván's, and it is small:** let the cron `git pull` land it (or pull manually), then run `sync_gbp_data.py` once and read `analytics_pipeline_health` plus a count per month of `gbp_search_keywords`. **The result that would close this: the 2026-08 row is present and non-zero, or is honestly `partial` with the month named.**~~

  ✅ **CLOSED August 31, 2026 — deployed, backfilled and verified on the box by Iván the same evening.** *`gbp_keywords`: `ok`, **34 rows written**, `detail` naming every empty month — `2025-02, 2025-12, 2026-02, 2026-03, 2026-06, 2026-08`. The 34 reconciles exactly against a per-month count of the table.* **`ok` rather than `partial` is itself the right answer and not a miss:** *none of those months held prior rows, so nothing was kept and nothing was destroyed —* ***and under the old code this identical run would have deleted six months while logging the identical line.***
  🚨 ***One thing this closed on is a diagnosis of mine that was WRONG, and it is struck rather than deleted.*** *I proposed that the four months missing entirely (2025-12, 2026-02, 2026-03, 2026-06) were an artefact of the window — "not that Google had nothing, but that nothing went back to ask after it did."* **The widened window went back and asked. Google returned empty for every one of them.** *The hole is real. The window fix is still correct and still necessary; what it bought was not those four months but* ***the ability to know they are empty***, *and to collect each future month once Google publishes it.* **The error shape is the same one the Gemini alarm was withdrawn for on Aug 30 — a mechanism proposed and stated as "likeliest" before anything went and looked.** *Get the number first.*
  ⚠️ **One live consequence for close #1, and it is a timing rule rather than a defect:** *`2026-08` is empty on an Aug 31 pull because* **GBP publishes a month's keywords after the month closes.** ***Do not quote an August keyword figure from an August 31 pull.*** *It self-heals — the window reaches the first of the previous month, so any nightly run from ~Sept 8 collects August once Google publishes it. Logged into `monthly-close-runbook.md`.*

It is trusted here because the digest kept arriving with other leads in it — a silent watchdog and a correct one would have looked identical.* *A stale copy of this item's own status was corrected in the same pass: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §8 still read «not yet observed on a real cron run».*

Verified only against a stubbed fixture. Needs: Iván commits + pushes so the dispatcher's `git pull` picks it up, then the next morning's digest should contain **zero** BR/AR lines. *Deliberately a one-line item, not a slot item — it self-closes on the first clean digest.* Detail: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §8.

- [x] ~~**The CoachMatch hire branch cannot parse a real TrainingPeaks email.**~~ **CLOSED August 20, 2026 — parser fixed, branch detection fixed, gate built and tested.** *Original item follows.* **The CoachMatch hire branch cannot parse a real TrainingPeaks email. Three stacked bugs, found August 20, 2026 from one misleading n8n error.** *First real CoachMatch hire to reach this branch since it went live July 24 — Andres Arechavaleta, Bronze, 20 Aug 15:04 local. It failed on all three.*

  ***Corrected the same hour, before Iván read it: the first draft of this item called it a crisis — "a paying athlete was not onboarded," 🚨 in the header. That was wrong and worth keeping visible.*** *Andres was already a Person in Twenty from the **CoachMatch lead** workflow, had already received the automatic email and reached out, and Iván had already flipped him to 1:1 coaching / WON, taken the intake form and started planning. **The workflow stopping did not drop him, because this workflow is not the only path an athlete reaches Iván by, and onboarding is still substantially manual.** The reasoning error: blast radius was inferred from the code path that failed rather than from what the business actually does around it. The bugs below are all real and all still open; what was invented was the consequence.*

  **Actual blast radius on Aug 20:** one blank duplicate Person in Twenty (`40ee57fb-784e-4cc3-a2e9-e0a4c230211a` — empty name, empty email, `customerType: OPT1_1_COACHING`, `leadStatus: WON_CUSTOMER`, sitting alongside Andres's real record), no `subscriber_tokens` row, and a stopped execution. **Delete the blank duplicate.** No re-run and no email — Andres is already handled.

  **(1) `Parse CoachMatch Hire` reads a field the real email does not have.** The template arrives as a **single `text/html` part**: the real message carries `"textPlain": ""`. The parser read `item.json.textPlain`, matched nothing, returned `parse_ok: false` with every field blank. *Second defect in the same node, invisible until the first is fixed:* `item.json.date` is **RFC-2822** (`Thu, 20 Aug 2026 18:04:37 +0000`), not ISO, so `.slice(0, 10)` produced **`"Thu, 20 Au"`** as `sign_up_date` — headed straight for Twenty's `signUpDate`. **Fixing the regex alone would have swapped a loud failure for a quiet one.**

  **(2) `Normalize Coaching Athlete` detected the branch with `Boolean(d.package_name)`** — the one field a failed CoachMatch parse is *guaranteed* to empty. The broken item was therefore **reclassified as a PRIVATE signup** and written to Twenty as one. That is why the junk record has `leadSource: null` instead of `COACHMATCH` and both dates set to the run date. A parse failure changing code path is the part that makes this hard to see.

  **(3) `parse_ok` was computed on Aug 8 and never read by anything.** Two mentions in the whole workflow JSON, both inside the node that sets it. `Telegram - Alta 1a1 Fallida` did not fire either — it only covers a Twenty **create error**, and the create *succeeded*. **Nothing alerted; the only reason this surfaced is that the workflow happened to die two nodes later.**

  ***Root cause of (1) and the date bug is a testing rule, not a parsing one: every payload this branch was ever verified against was hand-authored.*** *`athlete-onboarding-build-log.md` lines 392–416 and `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` lines 228–236 — all six mocks carry a `textPlain` key and an ISO `date`, because a person typed them. **A hand-written fixture agrees with whatever the code already assumes; that is its whole failure mode.** The branch had no real signup for 27 days, so the mock was the only evidence there was. **Standing rule: a mock payload for an email-triggered branch is captured from a real IMAP execution's node output, or it is not evidence.** Pin the trigger, copy the output JSON, paste it into the doc.*

  **How it presented, worth knowing for next time:** the error was `there is no parameter $4` on `Insert Coaching Token in Postgres` — a parameter that was fine. n8n's Postgres node **deletes** empty query parameters rather than passing NULL, so a blank `$2` shortens the array and the error names the highest placeholder. Mechanism and the general rule: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §21, Aug 20 addendum.

  **Repo side, done Aug 20:** `Parse CoachMatch Hire` and `Normalize Coaching Athlete` rewritten in `automation/subscription-lifecycle-automation.json`. The parser prefers `textPlain`, falls back to stripping and entity-decoding `textHtml`, and parses the date through `new Date()`. Normalize detects the branch from `productType` (set by `Classify Product`, present only on the private path, so it survives a parse failure on either side) and carries `parse_ok` on both shapes. **Verified against four fixtures before writing:** the real HTML-only email → `Andres Arechavaleta / andresarechavaleta@gmail.com / Bronze / 2026-08-20 / sign_up_date 2026-08-20`; the legacy plain-text mock → unchanged output; an accented name via `&#233;` entities → `José María Rodríguez`; an empty body → `parse_ok: false` and a blank date rather than a truncated one.

  **Iván's side — (a), (b) and (c) all closed August 20, 2026, same session.** *(a) blank duplicate Person deleted. (b) both Code nodes pasted into the live workflow. (c) `Build Welcome Email`'s `FORM_URL` was **already correct live** (`https://forms.gle/PcuUBUgRJQ5v6PDB7`) — **the drift was in the repo, not in n8n**: the reference copy had carried the `PEGAR_AQUI` placeholder since Aug 8 while the live node was fine, so restoring the repo copy into n8n would have broken the branch at its last step. Mirrored into the reference JSON the same session. Do not re-raise `FORM_URL`.*

  **Gate built and tested live, August 20, 2026 — the item is closed.** `Parse OK? (Coaching)` (IF, `{{ $json.parse_ok && Boolean($json.email) }}` boolean-is-true) sits between `Normalize Coaching Athlete` and `Check Existing Person (Coaching)`; true continues the existing flow untouched, false goes to `Telegram - Parseo Fallido` and stops. **Verified with a deliberately reworded body on execution 817**: 🚨 Telegram fired reading `Canal: COACHMATCH`, `Email detectado: (vacio)` — and **nothing was written to Twenty**. The same payload before the gate created a blank won-customer record. Both nodes and the rewired connections mirrored into `automation/subscription-lifecycle-automation.json` (54 nodes; node positions there are approximate, n8n laid them out).

  *Unverified live, deliberately: the **positive** path. The parser rewrite and the RFC-2822 date fix were proved against four fixtures in a sandbox, not against a real run — the only clean way to test it live writes a real Person, a real token and a real email. **The next real CoachMatch hire is the first live exercise of `sign_up_date`**, and if the date fix were wrong it would fail loudly at the Twenty write rather than silently, which is why this was an acceptable place to stop.*

  *Open question, small: `Parse New Subscription Email` and `Parse Churn Email` read `textPlain` the same way. All-Access has been working live so that template does have a plain part today — same single point of failure, and one of those emails carries money.*

- [x] ~~**Two bugs on the coaching-token insert that have been cancelling each other out.**~~ **CLOSED August 20, 2026 — both, confirmed live. Closing note at the end; the original item follows so the reasoning survives.** *(Found Aug 10, 2026, chasing the cosmetic one.)*
  **(a) The dangerous one — `NULLIF` into a `NOT NULL` column.** `Insert Coaching Token in Postgres` writes `VALUES ($1, $2, $3, NULLIF($4, 'UNKNOWN'), TRUE)` against `preferred_language TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'SPANISH'`. If `$4` ever genuinely equals `UNKNOWN`, `NULLIF` yields NULL, the insert violates the constraint, and a new coaching athlete ends up with a Twenty record and a welcome email but **no members-area token**. **The `DEFAULT` does not rescue it** — a default applies only when the column is *absent* from the INSERT, never when it is present and NULL. **This is the same trap already written down in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §21** from the intake pipeline, hit a second time in a different workflow, which is the argument for treating it as a rule rather than an anecdote.
  **(b) The cosmetic one that has been hiding it.** Every value reaching that node has arrived as `=UNKNOWN` — n8n's expression prefix leaking into a literal — which is not equal to `'UNKNOWN'`, so `NULLIF` passed it through untouched. Fix either bug alone and the failure in (a) appears for the first time. Fix (a) first.
  **Fix for (a):** `COALESCE(NULLIF($4, 'UNKNOWN'), 'SPANISH')`, which makes the database agree with the Spanish-fallback decision already implemented in `Build Welcome Email` (Iván's call, Aug 8). Apply the same to `Insert Token in Postgres` (All-Access), which has a bare `$4` and would store a literal `UNKNOWN`.
  **Fix for (b):** the repo's reference copy of the node is clean, so **the live workflow has drifted from it** and the source can't be identified from the repo. Bisect with one run: trigger a coaching signup for a person with no `preferredLanguage` in Twenty and read the output of `Coaching Athlete Upserted` — `=UNKNOWN` there means it's that Set node's value field; plain `UNKNOWN` there means the `=` is introduced at the Postgres node's `queryReplacement`.

  ***Update, August 12, 2026 — repo side done, n8n side is Iván's. And the fix changed shape.*** *Both nodes in `automation/subscription-lifecycle-automation.json` now read `CASE WHEN $4 IN ('SPANISH', 'ENGLISH', 'PORTUGUESE') THEN $4 ELSE 'SPANISH' END`, **not** the `COALESCE(NULLIF($4,'UNKNOWN'), 'SPANISH')` prescribed above. `COALESCE(NULLIF(...))` blacklists one known-bad string: it would have caught `'UNKNOWN'` and passed `=UNKNOWN` — the value actually arriving — straight through. A whitelist accepts the three values that exist and sends everything else to Spanish, which is also what `auth_service/app.py` already does on read; previously the column and the code disagreed about what an unrecognised language means. **Generalised in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §21: a sentinel value only works if you control every producer of that field — and the `=` leak proves we don't.** The infra doc's original §21 entry recorded the `NULLIF` sentinel as the fix, so it was teaching the bug; corrected in place. **Still needs Iván to paste both node changes into the n8n UI — the repo copy is documentation, not the source.***

  ***CONFIRMED LIVE, August 12, 2026 — (b) is not cosmetic. It is a language-routing bug affecting every non-Spanish coaching athlete, and it is now the highest-priority item on this branch.*** *Two-run test, pinned trigger data, real execution.*
  ***Run 1*** *(person absent from Twenty → expression falls back to `'UNKNOWN'`): the Telegram printed `Idioma: =UNKNOWN`. The template is `preferredLanguage === 'UNKNOWN' ? 'FALTA…' : preferredLanguage`, so printing the raw value **proves the strict comparison failed** — the value is `'=UNKNOWN'`, not `'UNKNOWN'`.*
  ***Run 2*** *(same person, `preferredLanguage` set to `ENGLISH` in Twenty by hand first): `Check Existing Person (Coaching)` returned `"preferredLanguage": "ENGLISH"` — confirmed in the node output — and the row written to `subscriber_tokens` came out **`SPANISH`**. **The `=` leaks on real values, not only on the `UNKNOWN` fallback.***
  ***The source is `Coaching Athlete Upserted`, assignment #3, and nothing else.*** *The decisive evidence is in the same Telegram message: `Lead source:` printed `FALTA -- ponelo en Twenty`, which requires `leadSourceExisting === 'UNKNOWN'` to be **true**. Assignments #3 and #4 sit in the same Set node and are written in identical syntax — #3 is corrupted, #4 is clean. So this is not n8n-wide behaviour and not the Postgres node's `queryReplacement`; it is one field's stored value, almost certainly beginning `==` (n8n strips the first `=` as the expression marker and the second survives into the output as a literal).*
  ***Blast radius is wider than the members-area URL.*** *`Build Welcome Email` reads the same field, so an English or Portuguese coaching athlete has been getting **the Spanish welcome email** as well as the Spanish members home. Likely near-zero in practice — there have been no coaching signups through this workflow since it went live July 24, and Tischhauser's ENGLISH token came from the July 25 backfill script, not from here — but that must be **verified by query, not assumed**, and the verification is logged below rather than performed.*
  ***Note what the whitelist fix now does to this bug:*** *it converts a wrong value into a silently-plausible one. Before today the column would have held `=ENGLISH`, which is visibly broken; now it holds `SPANISH`, which looks deliberate. **That is a good trade for safety and a bad one for detectability, and it is only acceptable because the root cause is being fixed in the same session.** If the Set node is not fixed, remove the whitelist — a wrong value you can see beats a wrong value you cannot.*

  ***And (b) is probably not cosmetic.*** *It is labelled "the cosmetic one" above, which is true of its hiding role and possibly false of its effect. If the `=` prefix leaks on **all** values rather than only on `UNKNOWN`, a real English coaching athlete arrives as `=ENGLISH`, matches nothing, and is routed to the Spanish members home — silently, with no error, both before and after today's whitelist. Tischhauser is exactly that shape. **Add one question to the bisect run: what does a real non-Spanish athlete produce at `Coaching Athlete Upserted`?** That single observation decides whether this is a tidiness item or a live routing bug, and it costs nothing extra.*
  *Standing note this reinforces: the reference JSON in `automation/` is documentation, not the source of truth. When it disagrees with live, live is right and the repo copy is stale — which is exactly why a bug can look absent in the repo and be present in production.*

  ***CLOSED August 20, 2026 — both bugs, confirmed live, from two independent pieces of evidence produced by a different investigation.***
  ***(a) the whitelist is live on the coaching node.*** *Not asserted from the repo: today's n8n error printed its own `Failed query`, and it reads `CASE WHEN $4 IN ('SPANISH', 'ENGLISH', 'PORTUGUESE') THEN $4 ELSE 'SPANISH' END`. The live node says so itself.*
  ***(b) the `=` leak on `Coaching Athlete Upserted` assignment #3 is fixed.*** *Confirmed by Iván, and corroborated in the same execution's node input: `"preferredLanguage": "UNKNOWN"` — clean, no leading `=`. On Aug 12 the identical fallback path produced `=UNKNOWN`. **The predicted failure in (a) never appeared, because (b) was fixed first and the whitelist absorbs `UNKNOWN` by design** — the two really were cancelling, and both are now gone rather than one.*
  ***The bisect prescribed above was never run and never needed to be.*** *The bug was located by reading which of two assignments in the same Set node printed a corrupted value in one Telegram message. Worth noting because the item carried a two-run live test as its plan for eight days: **the cheapest instrument was a message that had already been sent.***
  ***What this closes and what it does not.*** *It closes the language-routing bug for coaching athletes. It does **not** close the blast-radius verification, which was logged rather than performed and is now carried as its own small item below — the sentence "must be verified by query, not assumed" was written on Aug 12 and is still true.*

## Closed — August 13, 2026 (zone calculator: i18n, lead magnet, delivery pipeline, checkout audit)

*One long session. The members-area i18n branch's definition of done was met, the lead magnet it promised was built and wired, and a checkout audit at the end caught two live defects.*

**Zone calculator layout bugs.** Four public pages had three different left edges and a table overhanging the nav. Three CSS causes, all fixed and measured in a real headless browser. Detail: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §23. *Two of the three fixes were initially written into the file and silently discarded — a badly-closed comment — and both times the symptom looked like a specificity problem. **A rule present in the source is not a rule that applies; only the computed value discriminates.** That check now lives in `automation/layout-check.js`.*

**Sport pages lock their sport.** The picker stayed live on `/calculadora-de-zonas/<sport>/`, so switching to cycling on the running page left the 30-minute *run*-test explanation sitting under bike zones. `zcSport` now locks. *The wiring had been correct — for a different job, before per-sport pages existed. **A shared component's freedom has to shrink to match the claims of the page hosting it.*** Decision 21 in `zones-calculator-brief.md`.

**Nav hierarchy.** The `Recursos` dropdown listed five flat items; the three sport calculators are now indented under the hub. *Iván's call, and better than the proposal to hide them — the four URLs were never the duplication problem, a flat list that refused to say how they relate was.*

**Lead magnet built.** 16-page guide, three sports, generated by `automation/build-lead-magnet-pdf.js` from `automation/lead-magnet-content.js`. Home doc: `lead-magnet-sesiones-por-zona.md`. *Aligning Iván's source drafts to `data/zones.csv` caught the swim zones written as fixed offsets from CSS — the easy zones were 10–25 s/100m too fast, putting every aerobic swim at or above threshold. **Fixed second-offsets are wrong in principle**: the same offset is a different zone for a 1:15 and a 2:00 swimmer. Percentages travel; seconds do not.*

**Delivery pipeline live.** `/api/zone-workouts` → Twenty + guide email + Telegram, cloned from `plan-lead-workflow.json`. Runbook: `zone-magnet-runbook.md`. Three bugs found in live testing: the duplicate branch suppressed the email entirely (deduplicating a CRM record and withholding a deliverable are different decisions); `Check Twenty` read `$json.TWENTY_BASE_URL`, which only worked while Config sat directly upstream, so rewiring killed it one node after the email sent; and the running plan block's empty state blamed the reader's language. ***Standing lesson: reference n8n nodes by name, never by position — rewiring a workflow rewrites what `$json` means for everything downstream.***

**Three languages.** Calculator live in ES/EN/PT on twelve URLs with localised slugs, `zonesUi.json` fully translated, three per-language guide PDFs. Released the three things held back pending translation: EN/PT article CTAs, EN/PT `Recursos` dropdowns, and the workflow's hardcoded Spanish PDF. Detail: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §25.

**Running goal→distance recommendations.** Zone to raise → race distance whose build spends its weeks there (VO2máx→5k, Umbral alto→10k, Tempo→21k, Zona X→42k). Iván's idea; it also gave Zone X the only concrete "what to do about it" on the site. Decision 22 in `zones-calculator-brief.md`.

**Checkout audit + product rename.** The guide email sent the **Spanish** checkout link in all three languages and quoted **US$ 39,99 to Portuguese leads** whose product costs 29,99. ES and EN products renamed so the checkout title matches the site's "All-Access". ***The rename then broke the subscription pipeline***: `subscription-lifecycle-automation.json` identifies products by matching the title string in two Code nodes, and the rename deleted both anchors — every new ES/EN subscription would have classified as UNKNOWN, with no welcome email, no members token and **no error raised**, because the payment itself succeeds. Caught inside the same 30-minute window; no live subscriptions affected. **Standing rule now in `pricing-and-positioning.md`: renaming a TP Payments product is a two-system change — grep `automation/` for the string before deleting it.**

## Closed — August 12, 2026 (sales transcript review)

*13 real converted-athlete WhatsApp transcripts (Dec 2025 – Aug 2026) read in full. Produced `sales-playbook.md` — the pre-sale and onboarding message library — plus one decision, one duplicate found, and a lesson about this file.*

- [x] ~~**How far ahead is the plan published?**~~ — **DECIDED, Iván, Aug 12, 2026, and recorded in `methodology.md` §6.4:** week by week before the first test, four weeks loaded ahead after it. **The trigger is the test, not a date** — that's the point where zones are real and the athlete's actual week is known, so building ahead stops being work that gets reworked. *Opened and closed the same session; it existed as an item for about an hour.* **It was the only service complaint two different athletes raised unprompted, and they had been given opposite answers:** Roberto Palacios, Aug 11 — *"No me ha llegado el plan de la semana"*, three weeks past his test, looking at an empty calendar, promised 3–4 weeks ahead *"así no vuelve a pasar"*; Sergio, May 16 — *"¿Cuándo pondrás la rutina para las próximas semanas?"*, told the wait is deliberate so Monday's feedback shapes the week. Both answers were defensible, which is exactly why it survived: neither conversation felt like a mistake at the time.
- [x] ~~**Test-hygiene message inconsistency**~~ — **not a new item; a duplicate.** Already open as `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §6 Stage 9 (*"lowest-hanging item since day one"*). Re-raised with fresh transcript evidence attached, which is the second-copy-that-drifts failure the hygiene rules exist to prevent. The evidence was appended to the original entry and the duplicate deleted. *Confirmed rather than suspected now: delivered three different ways in three conversations — "revisa las instrucciones del test" (Roberto P), two voice notes (Alfonso), a verbal explanation of ritmos (Christian).*

> ***The lesson worth keeping from this session, and it is about this file rather than about sales.*** *The review was written up as **13 open items, and cut to 1** the same day on Iván's push-back — he had spent the day removing items and this added more than he had closed. On inspection ten of the thirteen were never open loops. **Five were notes about habits that the new playbook already fixes in text** (the discount frame, the price menu, a wrong `{DEPORTES}` variable that shipped to a real buyer, the missing "what failed before" question, social proof sent as a greeting) — a habit to hold is a standing decision and belongs in the doc that owns the behaviour, not on a checklist. **One was the duplicate above. Four folded into items that already existed and needed a line, not an entry** — semana-ideal automation → Stage 9; the Loom video → the TSS/PMC explainer now in NEXT; the EN/PT library → the members-area translation pass; the All-Access→coaching downsell → the win-back sequence in the small slot.*
> ***A finding is not an open item.*** *Most of what a review produces is a decision to record or a line to append. Writing each finding as its own checkbox is how this file got to 50 KB in the first place, and the failure mode is not laziness — it is thoroughness pointed at the wrong artefact.*

## Closed — August 12, 2026 (small-items session)

*A deliberate low-stakes session: no branch work, five small items picked off the list. Two closed outright; three moved forward and stayed open with sharper triggers. Recorded here because the pattern repeated — **in four of the five, the item as written down was not quite the item that existed.***

- [x] ~~**Fix `automation/content-engine/SETUP.md`'s `tp-admin` invocation** — it still documents running with `CONTENT_DB_DSN` (a URI). It is now run with discrete `PG_*` variables, because the password contains `:` and `@` and the URI form is a live tripwire. (Promoted Aug 6, 2026)~~ — **done.** Corrected in **two** places, not the one the item named: the known `docker run` line in Step 4, and the Step 3 `--dry-run` block, which carried the same `postgres://` export and would have failed identically for anyone reading the runbook from the top. Each carries a dated inline note. Verified against source rather than assumed — `admin_service/app.py` prefers `CONTENT_DB_DSN` **only when set**, falling back to discrete `PG_*` keyword args, so the fix is to leave the DSN unset rather than to override it. Downstream copies in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` and `triaperformance-project-instructions.md` corrected the same session. *General rule now recorded in the infra doc: a secret containing `:`, `@`, `/` or `?` cannot go into a connection URI unencoded, and hand-encoding it is the wrong fix — it puts a mangled secret into shell history.*
- [x] ~~**Explicit `Cache-Control` in `automation/Caddyfile`** — long-lived + `immutable` for `/assets/*` now that URLs are content-hashed, no-cache for HTML. Versioned URLs already make correctness independent of this. Trigger: any time.~~ — **done, but not as specified, and the specification was the interesting part.** "URLs are content-hashed" is true of css/js only. Counted against a clean build rather than assumed: `/assets/css` 755 of 755 refs carry `?v=`, `/assets/js` 334 of 334 — but `/assets/images` **0 of 1037** and `/assets/guias` **0 of 6**. A blanket `/assets/* immutable` rule would have pinned every image and all five lead-magnet PDFs for a year with no bust mechanism, so a revised guide would never reach anyone who had already downloaded it — and the weight-loss nutrition guide in LATER is a planned new one. Shipped as four tiers: `immutable` for css/js, 7 days for images, 1 hour for the guides, `private, no-store` for `/members/*` and `/admin/*`, `no-cache` for documents. *The document rule is the one that mattered: `?v=` versioning only works if the HTML carrying those URLs is fresh, so the two halves are one mechanism, and the August 6 stale-JS afternoon was only half-fixed until today.* Matchers are mutually exclusive by construction so their order cannot be broken by editing — deliberately unlike the members-area gates in the same file, which do depend on order.
  *Also cleared up in passing, so it isn't re-found as a bug: the local `_site/` contains 24 pages with unversioned css/js and a `/guias/index.html` with no source. Stale build output from Jul 29–Aug 2, before the `v` filter existed. `_site/` is gitignored and rebuilt on the VPS at deploy — **it cannot be used to reason about what is live**, and a fresh build shows 0 unversioned css/js refs and no `/guias/`.*

## Closed — the big branch: 1:1 athlete onboarding flow (opened Aug 8, 2026 · shipped Aug 9 · moved here Aug 10 when the next branch opened)

Home doc: `athlete-onboarding-flow.md`. Payment → classification → Twenty record → members access → welcome email (ES/EN/PT) → intake form → Postgres → Gemini briefing → email + Telegram, with no manual step in between. Technical record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §12 addendum and §21.

**Closed August 9, 2026.** The exit path was the last gap: Private cancellation verified against a real email (the existing regex already matched — no change needed), and CoachMatch cancellation built from scratch, since it was never handled at all. Both tested live. Athletes are now churned, de-provisioned and sent a goodbye email automatically on either channel.

Two traps found in the CoachMatch emails, both recorded in `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §5: TrainingPeaks' *"Cancellation **Request**"* subject **contains** its *"Cancellation"* subject, so a `contains` filter would churn a paying athlete mid-service; and the confirmed email carries **no email address** — `Nelson Carrion ()` — so that branch matches by normalized name and writes nothing unless exactly one record matches.

The live bug the branch uncovered, fixed the same day: **the subscription-lifecycle workflow tagged every 1:1 coaching sale as `ALL_ACCESS`.** `Filter - Is New Subscription` matched on subject alone, and All-Access and Private coaching share that subject. Fixed with an explicit two-allowlist `Classify Product` node and a `Route by Product` Switch whose fallback alerts and writes nothing; CoachMatch, previously dropped silently, got its own branch. Blast radius zero — no subscriptions of any kind since the workflow went live July 24.

The home doc was split the same day: `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` (17 KB, decisions and live state) + `athlete-onboarding-build-log.md` (CLOSED, the build record). It had reached 96 KB, ~60 KB of it instructions for building something already built — the same shape `open-loops.md` was in before its own split.

**What's left is in `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §6** and is deliberately manual, except Stage 8 (persist the perfect week, and create `athlete_profile` with it). Not opened as a branch — see `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §21's closing note on why building a schema nothing writes to would be speculative.

Follow-ups this branch created that closed with it: `Error Collector (global)` set as the Error Workflow on **all** workflows (Aug 9) · the All-Access welcome email rewritten in ES/EN/PT (Aug 10), which also caught a placeholder `NOTE:` line rendering as the first line of the subscriber email — never delivered, since there had been no All-Access signup since July 24. *Lesson kept: a note-to-self inside a live template is indistinguishable from copy.* · the seven dead `Disponibilidad semanal` columns — decided Aug 9 to **hide, not delete**: nothing reads them (`e.namedValues` comes from the form submission, not the sheet), those 51 rows are the evidence base for the §3.5 redesign, and deleting columns on a Forms-linked sheet is the operation most likely to disturb the linkage.

- [x] ~~**Confirm the exact name on the Stryd certificate.**~~ **CLOSED August 27, 2026, the same session it was opened.** Iván supplied the badge files and the answer: the mark reads **Certified Stryd Coach**, and the certification behind it is the **Stryd Power Coaching Course**. *Both are recorded in `site/_data/credentials.json` rather than one replacing the other — the mark is the credential name because it is what a reader recognises and what the badge itself says; the course is the note because it is what he actually completed.* **A second question was answered and is the more useful half:** he is listed at `stryd.com/coaches`, but that page opens a sidebar with no per-coach URL, so ***nothing Stryd went into the `Person` schema's `sameAs`***. *`sameAs` must identify the person; a shared directory page identifies every coach on it, and a URL that resolves to the wrong entity is worse than no URL at all.* The item existed because the wording had not been traced to its issuer — which is the same discipline that settled the "partner" question, applied to a smaller claim.

behaviour.** *Ticking `[x]` felt like closing an item, so 41 of them accumulated in the live list — the archive existed the whole time and the rule was written down.* *Also added to the index: the mutability of the source is what picks the conflict rule.* **Geography is now a first-class dimension in every table** *(from the Aug 29 affiliate scoping — which marketplace can be monetised at all depends on it)*, and two design decisions were made* ***unrepresentable rather than remembered***: *position is stored additively so that averaging an average is impossible, and a five-column `analytics_sync_log` exists so that a dead cron and a genuine zero cannot be confused — which is this pipeline’s most expensive possible failure.* **Nothing here is done: Iván has nine setup steps and one verification query, and the definition of done is what that query actually returns.** Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §9. *Prior: August 27, 2026* — **the authority layer shipped (small slot): credentials, three About pages in ES/EN/PT, and the site's first `Person` structured data.** *Raised by Iván as a question — "I am a TrainingPeaks partner, they send me most of the Latin American leads, are we leveraging that enough on the website?" — and the grep answered no, with a sharper miss than "not enough": the word* ***partner appeared exactly once on the whole site, inside the blurb an athlete copy-pastes on `/referidos/`.*** *The only place the relationship did credibility work was a message Iván never sends himself. stage the page's real assets, not only the files you changed.*** *One thing was deliberately NOT done: the fifth mark wraps to a second line and could be made to fit by trimming the repeated "Coach"/"Certified" from the labels.* **Rejected — these are issuer-granted marks, and shortening one to win a line break is the same error as inventing "partner", only smaller.** ***Fourth pass, same day — the strip became a logo band and moved down the page.*** *Iván: the position under the hero was itself unnatural, which is a different objection from the previous one — addendum 2 fixed the block's weight, this fixes its place.* **Now after the testimonials, before the final CTA: athlete proof, institutional proof, ask.** *Four logo tiles grouped by* ***issuing body*** *rather than by credential, because TrainingPeaks issued two of the five and showing its mark twice reads as a mistake.
