Two items opened off the audit script's first real run: the members-auth compose build context (**the Aug 10 fix did not take**) and rotation of the `analytics` Postgres password, exposed in a chat transcript.

**September 1, 2026 — the first Ahrefs crawl acted on, and closed the same session (small slot).** Five exports, **two real problems**: two broken internal links and 59 linked images without alt text. Fixed in `site/blog/metodo-noruego-ciclismo-entrenamiento-umbral.njk`, `site/blog/mito-ftp-estatico-ciclismo.njk`, `site/blog/plan-entrenamiento-maraton-lesionado.njk` and `site/_includes/partials/post-card.njk`. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §37. **Three things worth carrying forward.** *(1)* ***Both broken links were plausible rather than random***, *which is why nothing caught them: `cycling` is a real path segment (under `/en/`) and `/coaching/` is a page this site plainly ought to have. A reviewer reading the sentence would not stop on either.* *(2)* **The alt-text finding was a false positive that was still worth acting on** — `alt=""` on a decorative image inside a link with its own text is the WCAG-correct answer, and Ahrefs flags it anyway. ***Distinguishing "the tool is wrong" from "do nothing" is the judgement here***, *and the reasoning went into the partial's own comment block rather than into this file, because the person tempted to revert it will be reading the partial.* *(3)* ***The fix that matters is the test, not the three links.*** *The links were a symptom of an agent that writes URLs without being able to check them, and the check cannot live in the prompt — only the build knows what exists.* **Open, and it is Iván's:** *commit, push, let the deploy cron pull; then re-run the Ahrefs audit to confirm the two 404s and the 59 images clear.*

**August 31, 2026 — the drawer rebuild's own bug, found on a real iPhone (small slot).** Iván: *"if I click Planes, the sub menu opens, if I click it again it does not close. Same with Coaching"* — plus, in a narrowed Chrome window, groups opening on hover and shoving the next item out from under the cursor. **Two reports, one unscoped line of CSS**: `.nav-dropdown:hover .nav-dropdown-menu { display: block }` was written for the desktop dropdown and never gated to it. On iOS a tap latches a *sticky* `:hover`, and that latched rule at specificity (0,3,0) outranked the drawer's `display: none` at (0,1,2) — ***so the second tap did remove `.open` and did set `aria-expanded="false"`, and was simply outvoted. The handler was never broken.*** Fixed by gating hover-open on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` **and** `min-width: 861px`, with tap-to-open covering everything that gate excludes. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §35 addendum. **Three things worth carrying forward.** *(1)* **When a behaviour is split across CSS and JS, the two conditions have to be the same condition.** The bug was exactly a JS handler that stopped at 861px against a CSS rule that did not — and the obvious fix, a stronger selector, would have left the narrow-desktop symptom completely untouched. Reading both reports as one bug is what produced the right fix. *(2)* ***The Aug 26 session verified this component in a real headless browser, across five viewports, and still missed it — because every check was a mouse check.*** Touch was never emulated, so the one interaction mode the bug lived in was the one never exercised. The suite now runs iPhone, narrow-mouse, desktop and iPad-landscape — the last covering a case neither report mentioned: wide *and* touch, where a width-only gate would have made `menuOnly` "Recursos" a dead label. *(3)* ***A failing test was wrong, not the code*** — "1st tap on Coaching navigates" failed because `nav.json` had changed underneath it on Aug 27 (§36 gave Coaching a dropdown), so the assertion described a nav that no longer existed. *A stale assertion fails identically to a regression.* **One invariant recorded in `nav.json` as a result:** a dropdown parent with a `url` of its own must repeat that url as one of its children, because the parent row does not navigate on mobile. All three languages satisfy it today — check it whenever a nav item gains children.

**August 26, 2026 — mobile nav drawer rebuilt (small slot; the referral branch is untouched).** Raised by Iván from a screenshot of the open drawer: *"I don't like how it looks."* Measured first, in a real headless browser: **1368px of content in an 844px viewport**, because both dropdowns were permanently expanded and every one of the 17 rows was 17px with a 20px margin — *Contacto* and the language switcher were both below the fold of a panel that gave no sign it scrolled. Fixed with collapsed accordion groups, a type scale that distinguishes a heading from its own children, padding-based 44px rows, and a near-full-width panel. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §35. **Three things are worth carrying forward, and only the first is about the menu.** *(1)* **Two of the four causes were not cosmetic.** `header nav` was never `display: flex` — it has carried `flex-direction: column` and `align-items: flex-start` for months, both inert, and the bug stayed invisible because block layout produced the same picture the flex properties were asking for. And the overlay could never dim the header row: it sat outside `.site-nav-sticky`, and **`position: sticky` always creates a stacking context regardless of `z-index`**, so no z-index would have fixed it — the fix was moving one line of markup inside. *(2)* **The drawer had no close button.** The hamburger is a non-positioned child of the same stacking context, so the open panel painted over it; the only exit was a 70px strip of overlay. That is not a styling defect, and it survived every previous look at this component because the person looking already knew where the strip was. *(3)* **`members.css` was keeping its own 260px copy of the same drawer at a different breakpoint** — deleted, members pages now inherit the one component. *This project's one-owner-per-figure rule applied to CSS: the copy was three properties adrift and nobody had noticed.* ***The lesson is §23's, again, in a new place: a rule present in the source is not a rule that applies. Only the computed value — or in this case, a `margin-top: auto` that visibly did nothing — discriminates.***

**August 25, 2026 — blog listing rebuilt, and a database/repo drift bug found.** The listing went from a single column of text teasers to a filterable three-up card grid with a lead card and topic pills, in all three languages. Three things are worth carrying forward. **(1) The half-width layout was one line** — `.post-teaser { max-width: 65ch }`, a *reading* measure applied to a *listing*; correct on article prose, wrong on something that is scanned rather than read. **(2) `category` could never have driven the pills**, which is why a closed `topic` vocabulary now exists: the writer had invented **35 distinct category values across 60 articles** in three languages. `category` stays as the article page's fine-grained label; `topic` is the structural key, backfilled by `transKey` group so siblings cannot disagree. A 9th topic, `weight-loss`, was added against a tidier 8-topic taxonomy on commercial grounds — those plans are roughly half of recent sales and would otherwise be buried under running and cycling. **(3) Stock photography is now permitted on blog cards only**, amending `brand-guidelines.md` §6 rather than deleting the rule; AI-generated athletes stay prohibited. Cards render a topic-coloured panel when no image exists, so the grid was finished on day one and photos are an upgrade rather than a dependency.
***The bug is the more valuable finding, and it is this project's own hygiene rule applied to code: one owner per fact.*** *Piece 12 was published correctly by n8n on Aug 6, then **deleted from the repo on Aug 20 in a commit about the athlete flow** — swept up in unrelated work. Nothing told the database. It kept reporting `PUBLISHED`, and on **Aug 24 `auto_translate`, which treats `PUBLISHED` as ground truth, spent two generations producing EN and PT siblings of an article that had been off the site for four days** — siblings that went live declaring an hreflang group with a hole in it. **The repo owns whether an article exists; the database owns whether it is meant to; nothing checked they agreed.** `notify.py` now walks every `PUBLISHED` piece's `file_path` and reports drift daily until it is resolved, alongside the failed-agent and stale-agent checks. Deliberately one-directional — files without a database row are the six hand-written articles and reporting them would be noise.*

**Third session, Aug 12 — no build, a regroup after 8 days away, plus scoping.** The content engine had run unattended the whole time with every cron `OK` and nothing broken; the backlog was purely Iván's two review gates. Items logged, none started: **GSC/GA4 instrumentation** for the feedback agent (LATER — including why an MCP connector is the wrong mechanism: it would give *Claude in a conversation* the data, not the cron agents that need it), **content engine V2** for reducing the two review gates (LATER — translation auto-approve first, because translation is the only stage with a ground truth to compare against), **distribution** as the unbuilt half (LATER), and two small verifications from the Aug 4 session's untested tail plus a five-minute GSC indexing check (small slot). *One observation of Iván's is doing real work in the V2 entry: the research agent predates the translator and still proposes EN/PT ideas for universal topics, which produces two English articles competing on the same topic.*

***The pattern worth carrying out of this session: in four of the five items, the item as written down was not the item that existed.*** *The `SETUP.md` fix was in two places, not one. The `Cache-Control` item's premise ("URLs are content-hashed") held for css/js and was false for images and the lead-magnet PDFs, so the prescribed blanket rule would have pinned all of them for a year. The prescribed `COALESCE(NULLIF(...))` would not have caught the value actually arriving. The audit script's own first draft reported `outside: 0` regardless of what it found. **None of that was visible from the item text; all of it came from checking the real artefact — a clean build, the schema, the source file, a fixture.** For small items specifically, the temptation is to implement the note rather than verify it, because the note is short and the item is "easy." That is exactly backwards.*

*Previous session, August 11, 2026 — no branch work.* One item added to LATER (weight-loss nutrition guide), and **NEXT #8 sharpened**: the B-lite call is now a *testing-and-zones consultation* on the top 20 sellers, and is logged as the trial run for the coach hire in LATER. Detail moved to `growth-roadmap.md` §Storefront decision 3, which also had a top-10 / top-sellers contradiction with its own Phase 3 — resolved to 20. *Previous session, August 10, 2026 (second session):* **new big branch opened: members area in three languages.** `/members/en/` and `/members/pt/` shipped as a skeleton behind the same single Caddy gate; login now routes by the token's `preferred_language`; an unvalidated `next` open-redirect was fixed on the way past; a ghost "Members Access" button added to the public nav in all three languages. No tools translated — that is the branch's definition of done, not its start. The closed 1:1 onboarding branch moved to `open-loops-archive.md`. *Earlier same day: All-Access welcome email rewritten in ES/EN/PT and tested live (plus a placeholder NOTE removed that had been rendering as the first line of the subscriber email), and four items closed on Iván's confirmation (D1 test record, both Gemini items, the EN Ironman flag check). Aug 9: 1:1 onboarding branch closed. Aug 8: branch opened, plus a knowledge-base consolidation + factual-correction session.* This file was 50 KB, roughly 87% closed items and session history; all of that now lives in **`open-loops-archive.md`**. This file is the live list and nothing else.

***STANDING DECISION, Iván, August 30, 2026 — the catalogue count is 328 and it is CLOSED. Stop re-deriving it and stop flagging discrepancies in existing copy.*** *Source of truth is the **TrainingPeaks marketplace profile**, which reads "Showing 328 plans" — not a re-count. `data/training_plans_inventory.csv` is a mirror; reconciling **which** 328 would mean checking plans one by one against the spreadsheet, and that work has no buyer.* **Why this rule exists:** *this number has been raised in session after session, for a different reason each time, and every raise spends Iván's attention on something* ***no customer is auditing***. *He can publish more or unpublish stale ones at any time; the figure moves and that is fine.* **What still applies:** *use 328 in NEW copy, because getting it right when writing costs nothing.* **What no longer applies:** *auditing live posts, pages or listings for a wrong count, or reporting one as a finding.* *(The `321 planes` GBP post found today is an example of what is now out of scope — logged as history, not as an open item.)*

**Catalog count, for anything that needs it: 328 published plans (ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53) — and as of August 12, 2026 that is also the total number of inventory rows. Every row in `data/training_plans_inventory.csv` is now a published, live plan; there is no unpublished remainder to reason about.** *Worth protecting: that invariant is what makes "the catalogue" and "the file" the same thing, and it is the first time this week they have been.* Owner: `data/training_plans_inventory.csv`, `is_published=TRUE`. Do not hand-type it from memory — it has moved five times in six days. *(Aug 12, 2026: **303 → 327**, the largest single move yet — 24 plans confirmed live by Iván against TrainingPeaks and flipped. **Portuguese went 29 → 52, up 79%**, and is no longer the thin catalogue it has been described as all week. ES unchanged at 164. Aug 10, 2026: 301 → 303, the two EN full-distance Ironman plans.)*

Five items that lived only in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` were promoted here in the same session, because that doc's "Open items" section has been retired — it duplicated this file by design and had started to contradict it.

This file replaces the open-item lists previously scattered across `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md`, `growth-roadmap.md` (Sequencing), `content-engine-brief.md` (Open decisions), and the runbooks. Those docs keep the *detail*; this file is the *list*. Update it at the end of every working session.

## Agent-invented internal links (NOW item + NEXT #14) — closed September 4, 2026

- [ ] 🆕 **`npm test` reports 6 broken internal links, all invented by the writer agent in blog articles.** *(Found September 4, 2026, running the suite after unrelated work — **not** caused by it; no blog file was touched.)*

  ```
  /en/zone-calculator/cycling/      ← /en/blog/double-threshold-mistake-amateur-cyclists/
  /en/zone-calculator/running/      ← /en/blog/intensity-regulation-adjusting-threshold-paces/
  /en/cycling-zone-calculator/      ← /en/blog/static-ftp-myth-durability/
  /en/coaching/                     ← /en/blog/load-adjustment-rules-marathon-injury/
  /calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/    ← /pt/blog/erro-duplo-limiar-ciclistas-amadores/ + /pt/blog/mito-ftp-estatico/
  /pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/ ← /pt/blog/regulacao-intensidade-ajustar-ritmo-limiar-tempo-real/
  ```

  🚨 ***Every one is `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §37's finding repeating, and two are literally the same two URLs it fixed on September 1.*** *§37 fixed `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` — the English path segment inside a Spanish URL — and `/coaching/`, which has never existed. **Both are back, in different articles, three days later.*** *The English calculator lives at `/en/training-zones-calculator/`, the Portuguese at `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/`, and Coaching is the homepage anchor `/#coaching`, never a page.*

  **So the fix §37 shipped is working exactly as designed and is not enough.** *`tests/internal-links.test.js` catches these at build time, which is why they are visible at all — but it runs on demand, and* ***nothing stops the writer agent generating the same wrong URLs tomorrow.*** **The durable fix is upstream, in the writer's prompt or a post-generation link rewrite, not another round of hand-fixing seven articles.** *Note the failure is stable and cheap to prevent: the agent reaches for the URL a reader would guess, and there are only a handful of real ones.*

  ⚠️ **These are live pages with dead links in them today.** *Two jobs, and they should not be confused: fix the seven articles, then stop the source. The second one is the item.*

- [ ] **#14 · Seven broken internal links in agent-written blog articles.** *(Found September 4, 2026 by running `tests/internal-links.test.js` — the guard built in §37 for exactly this, doing its job.)* **All seven are links to zone-calculator and coaching URLs that do not exist**, invented by the writer agent inside article bodies:
  `/en/zone-calculator/cycling/`, `/en/zone-calculator/running/`, `/en/cycling-zone-calculator/`, `/en/coaching/`, `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` (twice, from two PT articles) and `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/`.
  ⚠️ ***The shape is the point: the agent is writing plausible URLs for pages it believes should exist, and four of the seven are near-misses on the real calculator slugs.*** *The real EN path is `/en/training-zones-calculator/`; there is no per-sport sub-path in any language.* **Two candidate fixes and they are not the same job:** (a) correct the seven links, which is ten minutes and fixes today; (b) give the writer the real internal URL set the way it is already given the plan catalogue, so it links from data instead of from memory. *(b) is the one that stops this recurring, and it is the same fix shape as the plan-card language check in §20.* **Nothing is redirecting these today — they 404.**

### CLOSED September 4, 2026 — agent-invented internal links, both items, plus the two classes the guard could not see

**Both items above are one item and they are closed together.** *The NOW entry and NEXT #14 are the same seven links found the same day, written up twice — which is itself the duplication this list keeps producing. Closed as one.*

**(a) The seven links: already fixed in commit `4774ed2`, before this session opened.** *Verified by reading the diff rather than the note: `/en/zone-calculator/cycling/` → `/en/training-zones-calculator/cycling/`, and so on across all seven articles.* ⚠️ ***One assertion in NEXT #14 was wrong and is worth keeping as a wrong belief: "there is no per-sport sub-path in any language."*** *There is. `/en/training-zones-calculator/{cycling,running,swimming}/`, `/calculadora-de-zonas/{ciclismo,running,natacion}/` and `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/{ciclismo,corrida,natacao}/` have all existed since the zone-magnet build (Aug 13). The agent's four "near-misses" were nearer than the item credited — it had the right shape and the wrong slug, which is a different and easier failure to fix than inventing a structure.*

**(b) The root fix shipped: `site_links()` + `check_links()` in `writer_agent.py`.** *This is option (b) from the item — link from data, not from memory — and it is deliberately the same shape as planCard, which has never produced a broken link because inventing one is impossible.*
  • `site_links(lang)` derives the linkable URL set from `site/` **at run time**: a directory holding an `index.njk`/`index.md` is a page, which is Eleventy's own rule. **That answers the objection recorded in `tests/internal-links.test.js`** — *"giving it a whitelist would not help, because the URL set changes with the build."* ***A derived set cannot go stale; a listed one can. The objection was to the wrong mechanism, not to the idea.***
  • **`noindex` is resolved the way Eleventy resolves it — directory data files, then front matter.** *Not a detail: the entire `/members/` tree is gated by one line in `site/members/members.json`, so a front-matter-only check would have handed the writer 40 gated pages and called them public.*
  • `check_links()` runs inside `validate()`, so a draft with an invented URL is **rejected before it reaches review**, for both the writer and the translator paths.
  • **Replayed against all seven historical failures: 7/7 caught. Replayed against all 116 published articles: 0 false positives.**
  • The translate prompt gained the rule that made this recur across languages: ***internal links are not translated, they are replaced.*** *`/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/` is what translating a slug's words looks like when the real page is `corrida`.*

**(c) A hand audit of every article and page found two classes the build guard was structurally blind to — both live at the time.** *This is the part the item did not anticipate, and it is the more useful half.*
  1. **Absolute own-domain links.** `site/blog/dolor-tendon-de-aquiles.njk` linked `https://triaperformance.com/members/aquiles/` — **a public article pointing a non-subscriber at a gated page**, which is the gated_teaser rule broken in the one place it matters. The test skipped it because it starts with `https:`. *Fixed: the routine is named in bold and the All-Access link beside it is now root-relative.*
  2. **Dead fragments.** Four plan-category pages had a "Ver planes" button on `#5k` / `#sprint` / `#running` / `#4-semanas` — **anchors deleted in the July 30 catalog refactor and never repointed.** *The page returns 200 and the button does nothing, which is why no crawler and no test ever complained.* And the members nav has shipped an FAQ item pointing at `#faq` on the **EN and PT** pages since Aug 10, where no FAQ section existed. *Fixed: the catalog section now carries `id="catalogo"` and the four buttons point at it; the EN and PT FAQ sections were written from the Spanish one.* ⚠️ **The FAQ copy is adapted, not new policy — every answer describes how the product already works — but it is customer-facing text that has not had Iván's eye on it.**
  🚨 ***The lesson, and it generalises past links: a check scoped to what broke last time only ever catches what broke last time.*** *`internal-links.test.js` was written on Sept 1 against the two classes Ahrefs had just found, and it was still passing 4 days later with a gated page linked from a public article and five dead buttons on the site.* **Now four checks, not two.**

**(d) One stale copy corrected on the way past.** `MARKET_NOTES["pt"]` in `writer_agent.py` told the translator the Portuguese catalogue "only has marathon, 5k, 10k, 21k and FTP" and excludes "swimming, HYROX or weight loss". ***It is the same false belief `/pt/all-access/` carried until it was fixed earlier the same day*** — the live PT catalogue is 53 plans across running (28), cycling (9), triathlon (8), swimming (4) and duathlon (4). **The note was steering Brazilian readers away from 22 plans they can already run.**

***Still open, and deliberately not done here: nothing checks EXTERNAL links.*** *There are none in any article today — the writer has never emitted one — so there is no live problem, only an unguarded direction. Worth a rule the day the first one appears.*


### Third write-up of the same finding, from the NOW list

- [ ] ✅ **Six broken internal links in blog posts — FIXED September 4, 2026.** *Found by running `tests/internal-links.test.js`, which is exactly what §37 built it for.* All seven files were content-engine posts inventing URLs: `/en/zone-calculator/{cycling,running}/`, `/en/cycling-zone-calculator/`, `/en/coaching/`, and — in two **Portuguese** posts — `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/`, *a Spanish path with an English segment, which is §37's original bug reappearing verbatim in a third language.* Every replacement was verified against the build.
  - [ ] **The writer agent is still inventing internal links.** *The test catches them after publication; nothing stops them being written.* **`tests/internal-links.test.js` is not in the content engine's publish path** — it is a repo test run by hand. Worth wiring into the engine's own gate, or the same handful reappears next month.
  > ⚠️ *Note for whoever runs that test: it reads `_site` and takes no override, so it silently checks whatever build happens to be on disk. It reported four already-fixed links until `_site` was rebuilt. Build into `_site` before trusting it.*

**CLOSED September 4, 2026 — and this was the THIRD write-up of one finding.** *The same seven links were entered here, in NOW, and as NEXT #14, all on the same day. `open-loops.md` was split on September 2 for exactly this and the behaviour survived the split.* **All three are now in this file, together, as one item.**

- ✅ **"The writer agent is still inventing internal links"** — *fixed at the source, not by wiring the JS test into the publish path as this note proposed.* **`check_links()` runs inside `writer_agent.validate()`**, so an invented URL is rejected before a human ever reviews the draft — earlier than the build gate, and it covers the translator path too. See the main closing note below.
- ✅ **The `_site` staleness trap** — *this note was right, and it cost time again the same day: the test reported 4/4 green against a build predating five live dead anchors.* **`internal-links.test.js` now refuses to run when `_site/` is older than `site/`** rather than answering confidently about bytes nobody is serving. ⚠️ ***That is the general shape of the bug and it is worth carrying forward: a guard reading a stale artifact does not fail, it lies.***

## Closed September 5, 2026 — the pixel `plan_id` gap

- [x] ~~**36% of marketplace plan views (1,055 of 2,930) carry no `plan_id` — a collection gap, not free to fix.**~~
  **CLOSED September 5, 2026. It was not a collection gap and it was free to fix.** *Of the 1,055 rows, **zero** carried a
  `price` and **zero** carried a trainingpeaks.com referrer, against 99.0% and ~71% for rows with a real `plan_id`.
  The query string was not partially lost — it was absent, so the request hit the bare Cloud Run root and was never
  on a listing. **Nine IPs produced all 1,055; one sent 1,008 of them in 90 seconds on 2026-08-31**, the day before
  close #1 read the table. Four such floods exist (2025-11-24 ×2, 2026-03-14, 2026-08-31), three running one pinned
  `okhttp/3.14.9`.*

  🚨 ***The belief worth preserving, because it is what kept this closed for four days: the September 2 note
  declaring the GA4 pairing "disproved" was itself the error.*** *It read: "the GA4 half closed as a **reading** bug…
  This half is a **collection** gap… one was free to fix and this one is not." **Both were reading bugs.** The
  pairing made in close #1 was right, and the note retiring it was wrong — and because the note sounded like a
  finding rather than a guess, nobody re-tested it. **The classification was reached by elimination and never
  segmented; one `GROUP BY` would have settled it on day one.**

  **Second lesson, and the more general one:** *`plan_views_clean` tested whether a request would **admit** to being
  a bot (`user_agent LIKE '%bot%'`) and reported the answer as validity. A spoofed Chrome string defeats that
  entirely — and `security-posture.md` F8 had predicted it in writing, in a Tier 3 finding nobody acted on. The
  structural test — a plan view names a plan — was available all along and costs one predicate.*

  **Four further defects surfaced in the same view, and the fifth explains why all four survived: it had never been
  in the repo.** *Catalogue sweeps counted as demand (14.3% of August); two of three personal IPs never excluded
  despite `open-loops.md` #2 claiming otherwise; `~~` is case-sensitive so `AhrefsBot` and `SemrushBot` sailed past
  `%bot%`; and NULL IP/UA rows were silently dropped by three-valued logic. Definition now lives at
  `automation/analytics/schema_plan_views.sql`.*

  ***Corrected figures:*** **August 2026 marketplace plan views are `1,604`, not `2,930` — 83% high.**
  *Nov 2025 → 1,164 · Mar 2026 → 1,663 · Jun 2026 → 2,708. `monthly-close/2026-08.md` and its metrics CSV corrected
  the same day; `triaperformance-growth-roadmap.md`'s median-views and view→purchase figures struck pending
  re-derivation.*

  ***Still open, in `open-loops.md`:*** *the collection-side fix (folded into the `plan-tracker-bigquery` runtime
  item, and blocked on whether the deployed source is the repo copy), and `audit-runtime-paths.sh`'s blindness to
  database objects.*

## Closed — September 5, 2026 · contact-form language branch

- [x] ~~**The contact-form confirmation email only fires when the Twenty write succeeds — repeat prospects get silence.**~~ **FIXED, TESTED AND LIVE — September 5, 2026 (Iván: "tested and green").**
  > **Two dead ends, both silent, both traced through the live connection graph:** `Already exists? ✔` *→ Telegram → Respond, **no email**; and* `Create Person ✘ → Is phone error? ✘` *→ Telegram alert → Respond, **no email**.* `Send confirmation email` *hangs off* `Person Created`, *which only exists when Twenty accepts the write.*
  > ***The returning-prospect case is the damaging one:** the visitor sees the success message in the browser, Iván gets a Telegram, and the person receives nothing.* **A repeat contact is a warm lead getting worse service than a cold one.**
  > 🔑 ***The fix has a precedent in this repo and it is Iván's own.*** `zone-magnet-runbook.md`: *"Deliberate side effect: delivery no longer depends on Twenty. If Twenty is down or rejects the write, the athlete still gets the guide and the only thing lost is the CRM record, which is the right way round for a lead magnet."* **The contact form is wired the opposite way round.** *Proposal in* `contact-form-pipeline-runbook.md`; *needs Iván's call before rewiring.*
  > ✅ ***Diagnosis closed the same day, and one hypothesis is dead rather than merely unlikely.*** *The failed execution's* `Check Twenty for existing Person` *returned* `{"data":{"people":[]},"totalCount":0,"pageInfo":{…}}` — **a well-formed Twenty response, so the filter works and `neverError: true` is not masking a broken lookup.** *The feared version — every repeat contact silently taking the create path — does not exist. **Do not re-raise it.*** *The rejection is the documented name-collision case (third occurrence), and widening the check to include name is deliberately rejected: `Juan Pérez` is not a primary key, and a false-positive dedupe silently drops a real lead.*
  > ✅ **The language branch itself works — Iván, September 5, 2026: three-language emails send and the zones-PDF link resolves.** *What is left is the wiring decision above, not the copy.*
  > ✅ ***The rewire shipped.*** `Config → Build confirmation copy → Send confirmation email → Check Twenty for existing Person`, *with the email node on **On Error → Continue (using error output)** into a new* `Telegram alert - email failed` *that rejoins the Twenty path.* **Both silent dead ends are gone: a repeat contact and a Twenty rejection now both receive the email.** *Accepted trade, same one `zone-magnet-runbook.md` already made: a double submission produces two identical emails.*
  > ⚠️ ***Two failures during the rewire, both mine to avoid next time, both now rules in the runbook.***
  >   **(1) `$json` is positional.** *`Check Twenty for existing Person` read `{{ $json.TWENTY_BASE_URL }}`, which worked only because `Config` was its immediate upstream. Moving the email in front of it resolved the URL to `/rest/people`.* **A rewire audit must cover data dependencies, not just the connection graph — grep the export for `$json.` before proposing any reorder.** *Five other nodes use bare `$json` and were fine; all read their immediate upstream and none of those links moved.*
  >   **(2) The `=` prefix is a mode marker, not a value.** *The fix was then pasted with its leading `=` into a field already in Expression mode, storing `==​{{ … }}` and producing* `Invalid URL: =http://100.70.89.17:3000/rest/people`. ***The tell is diagnostic: the expression resolved correctly and only a stray `=` was wrong — that is always a doubled marker, never a bad expression.*** **Quote expressions for the UI without the `=`.**
  > *Detail, and the rewire spec: `contact-form-pipeline-runbook.md`.*

- [x] ✅ ~~**Suppression list / unsubscribe mechanism.**~~ **BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE September 6, 2026.** Home doc: `unsubscribe-runbook.md`. *Two tables in the `members` DB (`email_suppression`, `unsubscribe_tokens`), a `GET /api/unsubscribe` Caddy route, a 7-node n8n workflow, confirmation pages and footer copy in ES/EN/PT, and `automation/mint-unsubscribe-tokens.py` — decoupled from Twenty on purpose, so the same script serves the members-area announcement and the referral asks.* **Proved on the real stack in all three languages** (1354 / 1353 / 1376 bytes, three different headings, three suppression rows, `used_at` set) **and used in production the same day: 30 emails to the Portuguese CoachMatch backlog.**
  ***This closes the August 13, 2026 item*** ~~"No consent/unsubscribe copy on either email-capture form… harmless for a single transactional send; it stops being harmless the moment either list gets a sequence"~~ — **whose own text predicted the moment it became load-bearing.** *The forms' fine print is no longer a promise with nothing behind it.*
  🚨 ***The build's real lesson, and why it took four import cycles: three defects, all in one node, and every one printed a plausible success.*** *(1) `respondToWebhook` at typeVersion 1.1 against an instance running 1.4 — real, but not the cause. (2) `Build Page` read bare `$json`, whose upstream is an INSERT node, so `email`/`lang` were undefined and the page rendered in the Spanish fallback with a blank address. (3) The same node was `runOnceForEachItem` while returning an array —* **HTTP 200, correct database writes, empty body.** *Every check that touched the write side passed;* `curl … | wc -c` *is what found it.* **Two standing rules came out of it, both in the runbook: read a row from the node that produced it, never bare `$json`; and byte-count a response before grepping it, because a grep that finds nothing and a body that contains nothing are indistinguishable.** *Same family as the `(not set)` GA4 sentinel and the `Ejercicio N de M` string — a verification that only touches the half that works reports success.*
  ⚠️ ***One trap found in production and written into the runbook: the token IS the identity.*** *A test recipient built by editing `email` while keeping a real recipient's `token` suppresses the REAL PERSON — the endpoint resolves the token, never the To: address. It happened on the first test send, was reverted in the database, and no mail ever reached the athlete.* **A test gets its own minted token, always.**
  *Split out and still open: the retrofit — six workflows send mail today and none check suppression.*

**Closed September 11, 2026**, on its own definition of done: *"a stranger reaches a plan from `/planes/` in two clicks, sees the members product before paying, and reads the story on `/sobre-ivan/` — and only then do race pages send traffic into it."* All four phases shipped — `/planes/` refactor, All-Access and the plan page, About, and **phase 4, the race landing pages: 19 races / 26 pages plus three generated guide hubs.** `storefront-rebuild-brief.md` is retired per its own instruction.

**Seven follow-ups did NOT close with it** and stayed in `open-loops.md` under *Carried forward from the storefront branch* — none of them blocked closure, and one (`/planes/duatlon/` and `/planes/fuerza/` have no hub) is why Phase 1's criterion 4 is recorded as half met rather than met.

*The block below is the NOW section as it stood at closing, moved intact. It is not summarised — the reasoning in these lines is the record.*

**Big branch: the storefront rebuild — opened September 9, 2026.** Home doc: **`storefront-rebuild-brief.md`**, which owns the four phases, each phase's definition of done, and the acceptance criteria. *(Iván: "I am committed to building a site that I am proud of.")*

**Definition of done:** a stranger reaches a plan from `/planes/` in two clicks, sees the members product before paying, and reads the story on `/sobre-ivan/` — and only then do race pages send traffic into it.

Detail: `storefront-rebuild-brief.md` §Phase 1.

Not dim: invisible, in all three languages, on the pages we had just spent the day making public.* **Cause: the dark layer said `.site-nav-sticky nav a { color: … }` and `Recursos` is `menuOnly` — a `<span role="button">`, not an `<a>`.** ⚠️ ***Third instance of one family in four days:*** *`.help a` beat a bare `a` on the auth screens; `.cta-final` declared a background and inherited a colour only right on a light page; and here a rule scoped to `a` could not see the sibling that is not an `a`.* 🔑 **Fixed structurally rather than by adding a selector: colour is set on `.site-nav-sticky nav` and inherited.** *Enumerating selectors converts the elements you thought of; setting colour on the container converts the ones you did not, including whatever is added to this nav next.* **1.15 → 5.81 in ES, EN and PT.** *Iván's report; his breadcrumb half was wrong — that was already 5.81 and fine.*

Detail: `storefront-rebuild-brief.md` §Phase 2 — the PDP rebuilt.

✅ **THIRD AND FOURTH PASSES, September 10, 2026 — the wrong-fit exit became a comparison, and a stale crawl found two real defects.** **`También puedes`: up to three `.catalog-card`s after the first prose block, ahead of the All-Access band**, replacing two text links at the foot of a bullet list. *A person on the wrong plan needs a comparison, not a whisper — "Ver la versión para Intermedio" carries none of the weeks, sessions, hours or price that made them reject this one.* **Same component as `/planes/`** *(`.catalog-card` lives in `plan-page.css`, which these pages load — checked first, because `.plan-card`, the obvious name, is defined only in the four sport-hub stylesheets and would have rendered nothing)*, **eyebrow carries the relation** *(siblings are same-sport-same-distance by construction, so the catalogue's eyebrow would print one string three times)*, **CTA is a link not a filled button**. **`ref()` in `plans.js` widened to the card's field set, still primitives only** — *that flattening is what stops plan A → sibling B → sibling A blowing Eleventy's data cascade.* **Coverage 303 of 328: 67 rows of three, 129 of two, 107 of one, 25 none.** ⚠️ ***Then Iván relayed a fresh crawl saying the alternatives were still missing — of a deploy that predated the commit.*** *Its "fixed" column describes the rail and the week object as live, so it straddles two deploys.* 🔑 **Working rule worth keeping: an audit of the deployed site is not an audit of the repo, and on a daily-cron deploy those are a day apart.** 🚨 ***Two real defects came out of it, neither the one it named.*** **(1) 71 of 328 plan pages printed the same number twice in the rail** — *41 ES, 22 EN, 8 PT plans are priced at exactly the All-Access figure, so the box read `US$ 39.99 pago único` above `US$ 39.99/mes`.* **The number now leaves that line on those pages only; 0 remain.** ⚠️ *The tempting fix — "por el mismo precio, todos los planes" — was refused: same number is not same price, and this is the page whose only job is a purchase.* **(2) The utility strip was still `--wash`, a pale blue band above a carbon nav, on the three PUBLIC core pages.** *The carbon version existed in `members-dark.css`;* ***the two carbon shells serve disjoint page sets and no page loads both***, *so the nav was themed in one file and the strip above it in the other.* **Same family as the Sept 9 nav rule, one level up: there a rule did not reach a sibling element, here it did not reach a sibling page.** 🚨 ***And the claim that did not hold, with the lesson attached:*** *the note said the carbon nav tokens were "not visible" and `Recursos` is "still dim grey".* **Pixel-sampled: 5.85:1, which clears §3.4's 4.5:1 gate — dim is what a secondary token is for, and dim and failing are different properties.** ⚠️ ***My own first reading said 3.8:1 and was wrong, because the nav is `rgba(--bg,.85)` and a computed-style walk composited that translucency over white.*** **Third wrong computed-contrast reading today against three correct pixel samples — stop computing contrast on anything translucent.** Detail: `storefront-rebuild-brief.md` §Phase 2 — third and fourth passes.

- [ ] 🆕 **One home for the carbon site chrome.** *(Opened September 10, 2026.)* **`.site-nav-sticky` and `.nav-utility` on carbon are now written twice** — `members-activacion.css` for the tool pages, `members-dark.css` for the 16 members pages — *because the two shells serve disjoint page sets and neither is universal.* **The fix is one body-level carbon class in `site.css`, which every page loads; `base.njk` has a bare `<body>` and no hook for it.** *Small, and it retires a duplication that has already produced one live defect.* ⚠️ **Until then, edit both files or neither** — both carry a cross-reference saying so.

✅ **THIRD PASS THE SAME DAY — the wrong-fit exit became a comparison, September 10, 2026.** *Iván: "I want the plan alternative offering to improve", carrying a second audit note.* ⚠️ ***Most of that note was auditing the LIVE page, which is the pre-rebuild version because none of this is pushed*** — *the rail, the week blocks and the single help CTA it listed as missing were already in the working tree.* **Checked item by item against the current build instead of answered from the ticket: H1 renders once, forms one, filled primary buttons three (Buy top, Buy foot, All-Access).** 🔑 **The new recommendation was right and is built:** *"¿No te encaja del todo?" plus two text links was a footnote —* **a person on the wrong plan needs a comparison, not a whisper**, *because "Ver la versión para Intermedio" carries none of the weeks, sessions, hours or price that made them reject this one.* **Now a `También puedes` row of up to three `.catalog-card`s after the first prose block, ahead of the All-Access band**, so the page reads as a ladder: *not this one → these three → or all of them.* **The same component as `/planes/`, not a second card system** *(`.catalog-card` lives in `plan-page.css`, which these pages load — checked first, because `.plan-card`, the obvious name, is defined only in the four sport-hub stylesheets and would have rendered nothing)*; **the eyebrow carries the relation instead of sport · distance**, *which would print the same string three times since siblings are same-sport-same-distance by construction*; **and their CTA is a link, not a filled button** — *three filled blues under Buy is the competing-CTA defect at a distance.* **`ref()` in `plans.js` widened from three fields to the card's set, still primitives only** — *that flattening is what stops plan A → sibling B → sibling A blowing Eleventy's data cascade.* **Coverage: 303 of 328 pages — 67 rows of three, 129 of two, 107 of one, 25 none.** *One sibling shows one card rather than being padded from another sport.* 🚨 ***And the audit's other surviving claim, worth the pattern more than the fix:*** *it said "H1 printed twice". The H1 renders* **once** *— but the breadcrumb printed `plan.displayName` in full directly above an `<h1>` printing the same 90-character string, so the page did open with the same sentence twice.* **The observation was right and its stated mechanism was wrong**, *which is exactly the case that has to be checked rather than accepted or dismissed.* **Breadcrumb truncated to 48 characters.** ⚠️ **Three things re-raised and NOT done:** *the TP banner PNGs (decided against on the files themselves, not an open question), a sticky rail (tried and measured away), and a "next distance" card slot —* ***that one is a real addition rather than a template change***, *since it needs a per-sport distance ladder (5K→10K→21K→42K, Sprint→Olympic→70.3→140.6) that does not exist in the data.* **Iván's call whether it earns its build.** *Also flagged, not touched: "Cómo funciona el entrenamiento" is the same 80/20 paragraph on all 328 pages — true, and it is coaching copy `methodology.md` owns, not a design call.*

**Order, Iván's:** **1** `/planes/` refactor · **2** All-Access refactor, including a way to see the members area from outside · **3** About with the real story · **4** race landing pages.

**Why ahead of race pages** *(NEXT #1, researched and ungated since Aug 6)*: race pages send traffic into this funnel, and **building the top of a funnel whose middle is a warehouse spends the traffic to prove the middle is broken.** Race pages get better the day this closes.

**The measurement that opened it, taken against the live site:** `/planes/` renders **164 cards on first paint, zero hidden, 18,737 px tall at 1920** (~47,000 px on a phone), no images. The plan page puts **Buy at y=556 and the email capture at y=625 — 69 px apart** — two competing primary actions — with zero images and a one-row "what you'll train per week" table. *The athlete then lands on TrainingPeaks and finally sees a photo, chips and a sample week.* ***The storefront hands off to the page that does the selling.*** ✅ ***All three halves of this measurement are now closed*** *— the 164 cards on September 9, the 69 px on September 10, and the empty plan page the same day. It stays here as the evidence that opened the branch, not as a current reading.*

## CLOSED — NEXT #1, the 42 km race landing pages (July 30 → September 11, 2026)

**Closed September 11, 2026.** Nineteen races, twenty-six pages (es 14 / en 5 / pt 7), three generated guide hubs, all passing `automation/race-page-check.js` and `npm test`. It was phase 4 of the storefront branch, so closing it closed that branch too.

**What it leaves behind, and the leaving-behind is the point:** `race-page-content-outline.md` carries **21 numbered rules**, each one a defect found on a built page rather than a preference — and the outline says so of each. `race-page-data-schema.md` owns the fields. `automation/race-page-check.js` enforces what a check can enforce, and the outline records the one class it deliberately cannot (a wrong-but-well-formed place name).

**Three open items did NOT close with it** and are carried in `open-loops.md` as the new #1: the `?race=` parameter, London's missing research, and the Portuguese line absent from `brand-guidelines.md` §8.

🚨 **The pattern worth carrying forward, from three build rounds and four external review passes:** *every single template defect was a field or a feature that nobody had exercised yet* — markdown rendering, lists, tables, `x-default`, the ladder bands, per-language place names. **None of them were wrong when written; all of them were untested when written.** *A field that has never been used is not a field that works.*

*The block below is the item as it stood at closing, moved intact.*

Nothing about this item changed; it is still fully researched and ungated.)* Planning done July 30, 2026: race longlist matched against `training_plans_inventory.csv` (only (distance × language) pairs with ≥3 matching plans qualify — kills EN full-distance and PT 70.3/full entirely, leaves PT as a marathon-only play), per-race data schema proposed (`race-page-data-schema.md` — plans are joined live at render time by distance/language/weeks-countdown, never stored as a static plan_id per race, so pages stay evergreen), 12 Tier 1 races fully researched and gathered into `data/races.csv`, and a page content outline written (`race-page-content-outline.md`). Full longlist + tiering + search-window calendar in `race-landing-pages-longlist.md`. ~~Trigger: plan template deployed live~~ — **satisfied August 6, 2026.** Nothing gates this build. *Iván's review corrected the inventory read: EN full-distance Ironman actually qualifies (3 plans, not 1 — **confirmed Aug 10, 2026: both flags were stale, now `TRUE`, so the EN full-distance race pages are unblocked with a complete difficulty ladder**), and the plan-matching join now also facets on intensity type (pace/HR/power) and a strength/gym add-on. California International Marathon (Dec 6, 2026) added to `data/races.csv` as a 3-language test case, to trial the full template pattern on one real race before the rest of the batch.*
  🆕 ***Six 42 km pages built and twice reviewed, September 11, 2026.*** *Valencia, Monterrey, Sevilla, Barcelona (ES/EN/PT where the catalogue supports it), Boston and Miami (ES/EN) — 10 pages, all passing* `automation/race-page-check.js`. **The nine corrections from the second review round are executed, and — the point of the pass — they are written down as rules rather than as fixes:** `race-page-content-outline.md` **§Corrections the first six pages earned** *(entry state beside the ladder; disjoint, place-named course segments; dual units on US races; no cross-page number citations; labels that don't restate their own body; a mandatory per-race `coach_hook`; `plan_note` where a rung doesn't mean what it normally means; the ample-phase sentence; a hero that doesn't restate block 1)*, and `race-page-data-schema.md` *for the four new fields plus the name-strip and Spanish-register rules.* ***Four of Grok's proposals were rejected with reasons and recorded as closed*** *(cap the ladder at three cards; split es-ES/es-419; strip figures from body prose; map volume band to a goal time) — do not re-open them.* ⚠️ **Two gaps stay open and are not defects of these pages:** `automation/register-sweep.py` *still does not scan* `data/races/*.json` *(voseo reached a shipped page through that gap), and the contact form has no field to carry a* `?race=` *parameter, so the CTA cannot yet tell Iván which race page sent a lead.*
  🆕 ***Segunda tanda: seis carreras más, 11 de septiembre de 2026 — el set son ahora 12 carreras y 19 páginas (es 12 / en 5 / pt 2).*** *Ciudad de México, Buenos Aires y Medellín en ES; Nueva York y Berlín en ES+EN; Río en PT+ES.* **Todas pasan `automation/race-page-check.js` y `npm test`.** *Selección: los dos mercados ES más grandes, los dos majors con más búsqueda en EN, el ancla PT, y Medellín — que se corrió el domingo pasado, así que su ventana de búsqueda para 2027 abre ahora.*
  ⚠️ **La tanda destapó cuatro defectos de plantilla que las seis primeras no podían destapar, y los cuatro están corregidos y documentados** en `race-page-content-outline.md` §«What the second six added»: **(1)** seis campos imprimían su propio markdown en crudo —perfil, nota de desnivel, clima, salida, inscripción, homologación, cajones— y *ahora hay una comprobación que rompe el build con cualquier `**…**` que llegue al HTML*; **(2)** faltaba una quinta fase, `imminent`, para una carrera a días de correrse (Buenos Aires estaba a nueve: la página decía «Faltan 1 semanas: ya estás dentro de la ventana del bloque de 12», que es falso además de agramatical) — y una página `imminent` muestra **la escalera completa**, no solo el bloque de 12; **(3)** cuatro notas de pelotón empezaban repitiendo el sello «Datos de la edición 2025» que la plantilla ya imprime encima; **(4)** el banner de coaching de Nueva York repetía el gancho del hero.
  ✅ ***Cerrado de paso: el punto ciego del barrido de registro.*** `automation/register-sweep.py` *ahora incluye* `data/races` *entre sus destinos por defecto — era una línea, y el motivo de que tardara un mes es lo aprovechable:* **el script ya sabía leer `.json` perfectamente; el directorio simplemente no estaba en la lista.** *Encontró cinco `acá` en la tanda nueva que dos lecturas a ojo habían dejado pasar.*
  📌 **Sigue abierto y no es defecto de estas páginas:** *el formulario de contacto no tiene campo para arrastrar un* `?race=`, *así que el CTA todavía no puede decir qué página de carrera originó el lead.*
  🔎 ***Auditoría externa de las 26 páginas, 11 de septiembre de 2026 — un hallazgo real, cuatro correcciones menores y seis afirmaciones falsas verificadas contra el build.*** **El hallazgo, y era más grande de lo que decía el informe:** `city` *y* `country` *eran cadenas sin variante por idioma, así que una página en inglés decía «Race guide · Nueva York», llevaba* `alt="Nueva York, Estados Unidos"` *y —lo que importa— metía* `"addressCountry": "Estados Unidos"` *dentro de su JSON-LD de* `SportsEvent`. **Seis de las doce páginas no-españolas estaban mal; las otras seis estaban bien sólo porque «Portugal» y «Brasil» se escriben igual en los dos idiomas**, que es justo por lo que nadie lo vio. ⚠️ ***La defensa es la forma del dato, no una aserción sobre el HTML: una cadena equivocada y una correcta son indistinguibles una vez renderizadas, así que `races.js` descarta una carrera multilingüe que no traiga `city` y `country` por idioma.*** *Escribir un check post-build que finja cubrir esto habría sido peor que no tenerlo.*
  *También de esa ronda:* **`Article` JSON-LD con autor** *(referencia, no copia: nombre, URL y* `sameAs` *hacia la página Sobre Iván, donde vive el* `Person` *completo con las credenciales —duplicar* `hasCredential` *en 26 páginas sería una afirmación en 27 sitios)*, **una firma visible bajo el gancho**, y **fechas en formato estadounidense en inglés para las carreras de EE. UU.** *(derivado de* `country.en`*, que sólo se pudo hacer una vez que el país supo en qué idioma estaba).*
  🗂️ ***Hub de guías + enlaces entre carreras — el punto más fuerte de la auditoría y el que cambia la secuencia.*** *Veintiséis guías sin nada que apunte a ellas son un clúster temático sin centro.* **Hub por idioma** — `/planes/running/guias-de-carrera/`, `/en/plans/running/race-guides/`, `/pt/planos/running/guias-de-prova/` — ***generado desde `races.byLanguage`, así que una carrera nueva aparece sola en cuanto existe su JSON***, ordenado por fecha con las indatadas al final, y enlazado desde los tres hubs de running. **Enlaces entre hermanas**, dos o tres por carrera, al final de la página: **editoriales, no derivados** — emparejar por regla (mismo país, misma distancia) produce vecinas basura; una persona eligiendo *las dos carreras entre las que el lector está decidiendo* no.
  ⏭️ **Esto tomó el slot de WIP por delante de los 21 km, a propósito.** *Diez medias colgando de un clúster sin hub serían un activo infrapromocionado más grande, no uno mejor — que es la regla de «distribución antes que creación» aplicada a lo que se acaba de construir.*
  🔧 ***Quinta fase de la escalera, `late`, 11 de septiembre de 2026 — el único hallazgo real de la tercera ronda de feedback de Grok.*** *Las bandas estaban partidas por el extremo equivocado:* `imminent` *cubría las carreras a días de correrse y todo lo que iba de tres a doce semanas seguía diciendo «ya estás dentro de la ventana del bloque de 12». A once semanas es verdad; **a cuatro —Lisboa, en producción— es la página contradiciendo su propia aritmética**.* **Umbral: ocho semanas, decisión de Iván, y es una posición de entrenamiento, no un número elegido al azar** — por encima, quien ya viene corriendo comprime las primeras semanas; por debajo, comprimir deja de ser comprimir. Vive en `LATE_WEEKS`, en `site/_data/races.js`. *Una página `late` **mantiene las tarjetas** y cambia la frase; se evaluó y se descartó subir el 1:1 por encima de la escalera.* ⚠️ ***Consecuencia que nadie señaló: Nueva York está a siete semanas, así que la página en inglés más grande del set entró en esta banda.***
  *De paso: el intro de planes decía «tres niveles y dos duraciones» en páginas que muestran una sola —ya no reclama duraciones, que las tarjetas las llevan en 22px— y se añadió una línea diciendo que TrainingPeaks cobra en dólares, **en los tres idiomas y no solo en portugués**, porque la ambigüedad es idéntica en Bogotá.*
  📊 **De las 11 observaciones de plantilla de esa ronda, 1 era el hallazgo de arriba, 4 eran correcciones menores reales (precio en USD sin etiquetar, la línea de «25% menos de oxígeno» repetida en Bogotá y CDMX y físicamente imprecisa, el aire de Santiago insinuado y no dicho, el CTA sin contexto de carrera —que sigue abierto—), 3 contradecían decisiones ya tomadas y documentadas (tope de tres tarjetas, alinear precios ES/PT, ocultar la escalera sin fecha) y 3 eran falsas y verificables en un minuto.** 🚨 ***«Schema still absent» iba por quinta vez: `FAQPage` + `SportsEvent` + `Place` + `Organization` están en las 26 páginas y el gate lo comprueba en cada build.*** *La causa es conocida y ya está anotada: los extractos de texto no muestran el contenido de `<script>`.* **Vale la pena mantener la proporción a la vista antes de la próxima ronda: el valor está en las observaciones de mecánica de página, no en las auditorías de infraestructura.**
  🏁 ***El set de 42 km está cerrado, 11 de septiembre de 2026: 19 carreras, 26 páginas (es 14 / en 5 / pt 7).*** *Última tanda de siete: Bogotá y Santiago en ES; Lisboa, Porto, Porto Alegre y las dos de São Paulo en PT — que pasa de 2 a 7 páginas y deja de ser el idioma de relleno del set.* **Todas pasan `automation/race-page-check.js` y `npm test`.** ✅ ***Y cierra el único ítem abierto del esquema:*** *Iván resolvió la ambigüedad de São Paulo —* `sao-paulo-marathon` *es la Internacional de Yescom (abril, Ibirapuera) y* `sao-paulo-2-marathon` *es la SP City de Iguana (julio, Pacaembu → Jockey). Nunca fue un problema de datos: las dos estaban documentadas, y lo único que faltaba era saber cuál de las dos fotos subidas correspondía a cada carrera.*
  ⚠️ **Tres defectos más, de la misma familia que los de la tanda anterior — una función que nadie había ejercitado:** **(1)** `raceProse` *solo renderizaba párrafos y negrita, así que una lista de cortes salía como una línea corrida con guiones sueltos y una tabla como un muro de barras; ahora renderiza lista, tabla y párrafo* (Medellín y Berlín llevaban un día así); **(2)** 🚨 **`x-default` se emitía solo cuando existía la versión en español**, *de modo que toda página del sitio sin hermana en ES —no solo las de carreras— se publicaba sin él; ahora cae en cascada es → en → pt*; **(3)** *el título en PT no cabía en el clamp de 60 caracteres porque su fórmula era más larga que la de ES y EN — ahora las tres dicen lo mismo.*
  📌 **Decisión de idioma que conviene tener a mano:** *Lisboa y Porto se escribieron primero en portugués europeo y se convirtieron a portugués de Brasil, porque el catálogo, la navegación y* `raceUi.json` *del sitio son brasileños. Mezclar variantes en una misma página es peor que cualquiera de las dos.* `brand-guidelines.md` **§8 no tiene todavía esa línea para el portugués como la tiene para el español.**
  📌 **Lo único que queda del set:** *Londres tiene foto y no tiene investigación — ninguna otra carrera del repo está en ese estado.*

### Open Loops — the single source of truth for what's in flight

**Last updated: September 10, 2026** *(storefront branch, Phase 2 — All-Access, then the plan page twice)*. — 🔸 **Two pages stopped explaining and started showing.** ✅ **All-Access reordered in all three languages** — product before maths, the first picture of the SKU at y≈1,300, the sixteen-card library grid cut to three rows. ✅ **A revenue argument that pays for itself:** *TrainingPeaks Virtual ships with Premium, so a cyclist already paying Zwift ($19.99) and Premium ($19.95) is spending* **$39.94** *— the ES/EN price of All-Access — with no plans in it.* ✅ **The plan page was rebuilt, then rebuilt again the same day.** *The morning gave it a dark All-Access card; the afternoon moved that card out of the first screen, because* **two prices ask a reader to pick a commercial model before they have decided the plan is theirs** *— and `monthly-close/2026-08.md` says the product being argued for is 3 → 1 subscribers at 27.3% NRR with the survivor never logged in.* **One price, one primary, All-Access as a sentence; the week is now an object instead of a table row; Heat appears on a plan page for the first time, on the longest session.** 🚨 ***The audit said to reuse the banner PNGs from the TrainingPeaks listings — they carry a retired logo and an uncorrectable "+1000 planes vendidos", and they exist only because that CMS allows four tags and 4,000 characters.*** 🚨 ***And the copy had called sessions "days" for a month*** *— impossible on 66 of 301 plans, and invisible until the week block put the rest-day count directly above the sentence.* ⚠️ **The plan-page half is NOT LIVE — 7 files uncommitted, 1 deleted.** *Which is also why the second audit read as a list of things not done: it was reading the deployed page.* **Iván commits, pushes and deploys.**

*Also September 10, 2026 — **cleanup pass:** six items ticked `[x]` and never moved, plus three closed or duplicate items, moved to `open-loops-archive.md`. Two of the ticked items had an open half, which stays here as its own checkbox (PT subscriber `access_count` read; applying `schema.sql` to the live `members` DB).* *Then six more closed on Iván's word: the WhatsApp "how did you find me" line (standing practice), `Recursos` page (won't build), the `/guias/` landing page, race-page research (done, pages in build), the knee-routine decision (yes), and `NEXT #16` (attribution off, reference JSONs mirrored).*

*Previously: **September 8, 2026*** *(routine-engine feedback session — the third entry today; the pace-converter session and the weekly hygiene pass are the first two `Previously` blocks below)*. — ✅ **both routine engines changed from one athlete's feedback, 25 paid pages. CLOSED — tested and confirmed live by Iván**, on the second deploy; the first one shipped new markup running August's JavaScript, which is the finding that outlived the four fixes and is in `open-loops-archive.md`. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §48. **(1) Every block now opens PAUSED with its cue on screen**, with hands-free chaining behind a remembered toggle. 🔑 ***The diagnostic worth carrying: the tool's default behaviour required a workaround on its own first screen, and the workaround was a control the tool provides*** — *when the obvious first action a user takes is to defeat what just happened, the default is wrong, not the user.* **(2) A duration prescription now gets a countdown the athlete starts.** 🚨 ***`strength-tool.js`'s founding principle — "a countdown tells someone to keep going when the prescription is eight good reps" — is right and was applied one step too widely: true of reps, false of a hold.*** *So the one exercise on the knee tool prescribed for its analgesic effect printed "45s" and left the athlete counting. ⚠️ ***The one thing still open from the pass:*** *~1.7 KB of inline build narrative per open item. Mechanical moves are done; this one needs a call per item.*

✅ ***Re-measured September 8, 2026 (hygiene pass), and the duplication half of that concern is closed — by the split itself, not by any later work.*** ~~*this file ↔ `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md`, the repo's hottest duplication pair (55 near-duplicate passages)*~~ **is now 4 passages. The pair at 39 is `open-loops-archive.md` ↔ `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md`** — *the same duplication, relocated: the September 2 split moved 44% of this file into the archive and took the duplicated build narrative with it, where it costs nothing because the archive is opened on demand rather than at the start of every session.* 🔑 ***The lesson is about measurement, not duplication: the 55 was measured on the pre-split 252 KB file, the split fixed it the same day, and the note describing the problem outlived the fix by six days because nobody re-ran the count.*** *A finding and its own remedy landing in one session is exactly when a stale note gets written.*

*Prior sessions: the twelve chained "Prior:" entries that used to live in this header —* **43.8 KB of session narrative** *— moved to* `open-loops-archive.md` *§Session log on September 2, 2026, together with* **41 items that had already been ticked closed and never moved (61.2 KB)**, **four items that each described themselves in their own text as duplicates of an item above them**, *and* **the entire August 14 status-claim-audit section**, *which its own opening paragraph had declared fully closed nineteen days earlier.* ***This file is the list of what is open. It is not the record of how anything got that way.*** *That was the Aug 8 split's rule and it had stopped being enforced: the file reached* **252 KB** *— five times the 50 KB that triggered the split — of which* **44% was history**. **Now 142 KB and 67 open items, zero closed.** ⚠️ ***The remaining 142 KB is not clean, and the number to watch is this one: 67 open items across 142 KB is ~2 KB of prose per open item.*** *Each item carries its own build narrative inline, which is why `open-loops.md` ↔ `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` is the repo's hottest duplication pair at 55 near-duplicate passages. **Moving closed things out was mechanical and is done; trimming open items down to a line plus a pointer needs a judgement call per item and has not been started.***

#### Rules

- 🔢 **EVERY item in this file is a `- [ ]` checkbox — NOW, Backlog, NEXT and LATER alike.** *(Standardised September 3, 2026, Iván. NEXT and LATER used numbered and bulleted items, so every count anyone ran — including a whole session's worth of "64 → 45" reports — silently described the checkbox half and read as though it described the file.* ***Arithmetically right, and it told you something false: the real total that day was 83.***) **NEXT keeps its numbers inside the title (`**#1 · …**`) because 27 references to `NEXT #N` exist across 15 files.** *Do not renumber; append.*
- **WIP limit: 1 big branch. That's the whole rule.** A new branch opens only when one closes.
- ~~**+ 1 small slot.**~~ ***Removed September 2, 2026, Iván's call — the section below is now `Backlog`, not a slot.*** *The slot was declared at 1 and held 9 on Aug 12, 11 on Aug 24 and 22 today; five consecutive sessions restated the count and offered the same three options, and none was ever taken.* **A limit violated 22-fold for three weeks was not a limit, it was a label, and the only thing it produced was a paragraph of self-reproach in every hygiene pass.** *His reason for removing rather than enforcing it: "I will do everything anyway" — which is the honest description of how this list has always been worked, and a rule that contradicts observed behaviour loses to the behaviour every time.* **Nothing is to count, report or apologise for the size of the backlog again.**
- 🚨 **An item is not closed until it is struck HERE. Doing the work is only half of it.** *(Added September 5, 2026, Iván — after two items in one session turned out to have been executed days earlier and were still being reported back to him as open: the All-Access `Insert Token in Postgres` check, and the Private 1:1 checkout URL, which `triaperformance-pricing-and-positioning.md` had carried since September 4.)* **This is the September 2 lesson at one remove.** *That pass found 41 items ticked `[x]` and never moved to the archive, and concluded that ticking is not closing. The failure here is one step earlier: work done in the world and never ticked at all.* ***So the test before reporting an item as open is not whether the box is empty — it is whether the owning doc still agrees it is open.*** *Cheap for anything with a home doc; both misses here were one grep each.*
- Every session ends with its branch either **closed** or **parked with an explicit trigger** written here.
- NOW = active this week. NEXT = queued, with its trigger. LATER = parked deliberately, with its trigger.
- When an item closes, move it to `open-loops-archive.md` with its closing note intact. Do not summarise on the way out — the reasoning is what stops the question being re-asked in six weeks.

##### Carried over from the storefront branch — catalogue strategy, not build work

- [ ] **Duathlon is the most under-built category in the catalogue.** Earnings per published plan by sport: **Duathlon $121** (15 plans), Strength $70 (2), Triathlon $66 (51), Swimming $41 (40), Running $37 (135), **Cycling $29** (60). *(Triathlon updated Aug 10, 2026 — 49 → 51 plans, $69 → $66, after the two EN Ironman flags were corrected. Ranking unchanged.)* Cycling has four times the inventory of Duathlon and a quarter of the return per plan. This says nothing against the new Ciclismo hub — 60 plans need a door — but it does say the next plans built should be Duathlon, not Cycling. Worth a session to decide which Duathlon gaps to fill. Source: `training-plans-analysis.md` §5. (Aug 6, 2026)
- [ ] **Run a pricing test — the site finally makes it possible.** *(Iván, Aug 6, 2026.)* Units are flat (160 → 170 → ~204 annualised) while average sale doubled ($21 → $49), so **price has been the only working growth lever and it has never been tested deliberately** — it drifted upward as the catalogue changed.
  **Do the free version first: elasticity from existing data.** 507 sales spanning $19.99–$79.99 already exist in `data/plan_sales.csv`, and per-plan view counts exist in the BigQuery pixel data / `plan_views_clean` on the VPS. Conversion by price band is computable today with zero risk. **Confound to control for:** average price rose partly because the *mix* changed (HYROX at $56 avg arrived in Aug 2025), so compare within a sport and plan family, never across the whole catalogue — otherwise the analysis just rediscovers the mix shift.
  **Then the live test.** TrainingPeaks has no split-testing, so a true A/B isn't available; two workable designs — (a) **sequential**: move one plan family ±20% for 8–12 weeks against unchanged siblings as control, using the same before/after window logic as the listing-rewrite experiment; (b) **matched pairs**: the same plan already sells at different prices across languages, which is a natural experiment sitting in the data.
  **Why it matters now:** a 10% price move worth taking is worth ~$600/yr on current volume and costs nothing to implement. Seasonality says run it outside Jan and Jun so a peak doesn't masquerade as a result. Trigger: any time; natural companion to the TP listing-rewrite read in late October.

- [ ] 🆕 **The project's Custom Instructions field goes stale by design — make it static instead of remembering to repaste it.** *(Opened September 8, 2026, from Iván's own question: "should I start every day with a copy paste?")* 🛑 **No. Daily pasting is the exact thing this project killed on August 12** *— twenty documents re-uploaded by hand every morning, removed because they cost his time forever, which is the test this project applies to everything.* **Rebuilding that ritual in a smaller box is the same mistake.**
  **The real shape of the problem:** *the mirror is edited by sessions, automatically, whenever a doc is added, renamed or retired. The field is edited by hand. So the gap opens on doc churn — roughly weekly — and never on a schedule, which is precisely why a daily habit is both too often and unreliable.* *Measured twice: 14 entries missing September 2, 14 again September 8.*
  ***The fix that takes his time once:*** **most of the field does not go stale — the working relationship, the guardrails and the hygiene rules have barely moved in a month. Only the KB index churns.** *A session's first act is already to open this repo. So the field can carry the durable half plus one line — "the document index lives in `triaperformance-project-instructions.md`; read it from the repo before answering anything about the knowledge base" — and the index maintains itself in git, where sessions already edit it.* **The field then changes when the working relationship changes, which is rarely, and the daily-paste question stops existing.**
  ⚠️ ***One thing to verify before doing it, because it is the whole load-bearing assumption:*** *that a session reliably reads the index from the repo when the field only points at it. Today the field carries the index inline, so nothing has ever tested the pointer.* **Trigger: any time. Until then the interim rule below stands.**
  **Interim, and it is two occasions rather than a habit:** *(1)* **whenever any session tells him a doc was added, renamed or retired** — every session is already required to say so; *(2)* **unconditionally after each weekly hygiene pass**, which is the only thing that reliably detects the drift. *Twenty seconds, once or twice a week, not daily.*

- [ ] 🆕 **`members_access_gap.py --expect` goes stale every week by design — decide whether it earns its keep.** *(Opened September 8, 2026, first live use.)* **It failed with `--expect 290, fetched 305`, and nothing was wrong:** *`--expect N` is an exact-equality gate (`len(out) != expect`), Twenty had gained 15 people since the number was written on September 5, and the CRM gains people continuously.* ⚠️ ***So this gate will refuse almost every weekly run, and a check that cries wolf weekly is the thing this repo has already written down twice as training people to skim.*** **The guard that actually catches the original bug is the other one** — *`totalCount` vs fetched, line 239, which did not fire, which is how we know pagination is healthy.* **Two options:** *(a)* drop `--expect` and rely on the `totalCount` guard, *(b)* change it to `--expect-min` so it catches an under-read without going stale as the book grows. *(b) keeps a second gate independent of `totalCount` — which was the point — without the maintenance. **Either is ten minutes.** Trigger: any time; next hygiene pass at the latest.

- [ ] 🆕 **`all-access.css` styles bare `h2` and `.section-intro`, and it leaks into every shared partial the page includes.** *(Root cause of the "Acreditaciones text only uses half the area" bug Iván reported September 4, 2026 — his words: **"this bug keeps happening"**, which is the part worth acting on.)*

  **What happened:** `all-access.css` line 33 is a bare `h2 { … text-align: center }` for the page's own sections. `partials/credentials-band.njk` is a **shared** partial designed left-aligned — label, lead, logo grid and "Sobre Iván" link all start at the same left edge — and it renders correctly on the homepage and the three About pages, *which do not load this stylesheet.* On All-Access the bare rule reached in and centred only its heading, over a lead paragraph capped at `65ch`. **Measured: every element at `left=204`, the h2 alone at `text-align: center`.** *A centred heading over a left-aligned half-width paragraph is what "only uses half the area" looks like.*

  **Fixed narrowly** with `.cred-band h2 { text-align: left; }` — restoring the partial's own design rather than un-centring the page, since the centred h2 *is* correct for this page's own sections.

  🚨 ***The item is the pattern, not this instance.*** *A page-scoped stylesheet using bare element selectors will silently restyle every shared partial that page includes, and the damage shows up on the partial — far from the rule that caused it.* **Same family as `members-home.css` `.site-nav` vs `.site-nav-sticky` (§41) and §26 before it: a rule that is correct for what its author was looking at, and wrong for what they weren't.** *Worth a pass over the page-scoped stylesheets for bare `h1`/`h2`/`h3`/`p`/`a`/`ul` selectors and scoping them to their own sections. Cheap, and it retires a recurring class rather than another instance of it.*
